# TODO-04 -- Page Screening on Muse Glimmer with Muse Spark Failover

> **Goal:** The impersonation monitor screens candidate pages through one `screenPage` interface: a Muse Glimmer 30B LoRA fine-tuned for this job on Fireworks AI (free supervised fine-tuning of Glimmer until 2026-11-30), served from a dedicated Fireworks deployment that scales to zero after 5 idle minutes and runs 30 minutes a day on average (one day borrowed ahead; operator, 2026-10-31 22:16 SAST, §34), with its warm-up counted against that budget, and Muse Spark in Standard mode on the Meta Model API (`store: false`, the Meta client and AI Gateway) whenever the deployment is warming past its limit, out of daily minutes, failing, timing out or over its cap, so the monitor never stops. The training set is built as large as quality allows from public phishing-page datasets (licence-checked), real benign pages rendered locally, the redacted sample mail in `samples/` (consent extended 2026-10-28) and synthetic South African lures. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (§8's completion review):** no synthetic lure is made; the operator removed §8 on 2026-10-28 (21:41 SAST), so the South African lures are the redacted sample pages (§7), kept in both splits by §10 and measured with their denominators by §13. Stamped §5's fallback item (raise §8's target to 3,000 when no source is accepted) is superseded: `sources.ts check` now fails outright when no source is accepted. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (operator decisions, 15:01 to 15:16 SAST):** the screener is a fine-tuned Glimmer on a dedicated deployment instead of base Glimmer on serverless (Glimmer is not served pay-per-token, checked 11:33 SAST, and fine-tuned LoRAs never run on serverless); the 30-minute daily limit, 5-minute scale to zero and warm-up handling are the operator's; the pilot client's consent is extended so redacted sample content may go into training lines on Fireworks (`samples/pilot-consent.md`, local); Astra (GPT-6 Astra through the Codex CLI) also collects public datasets outside the repository, Claude writes every guide and script and verifies what Astra collects, and Astra keeps reviewing Claude's code; general cyber security training is out of scope. The earlier Goal (09:05 SAST), kept for the record: Muse Glimmer 30B on Fireworks AI serverless first (through AI Gateway; the chat completions endpoint, which keeps no prompt or output data for open models, never the Responses API, which keeps conversations 30 days), and Muse Spark in Standard mode whenever Fireworks fails, times out, rate-limits or reaches its cap; Glimmer at US$0.35 and US$1.50 a million input and output tokens. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (operator decision, 09:05 SAST):** Fireworks' US$100 partner credit is loaded, so the 2026-10-27 removal is reversed and Glimmer on Fireworks is the screener again, with Spark (the 2026-10-27 screener) as its failover. Lookalike pages copy real people's details, the reason the Contributor plan was withdrawn. The Goal before that: one `screenPage` interface on Muse Spark (Standard mode, `store: false`), about US$0.005 a page. **Corrected 2026-10-27 (operator decisions):** this file planned Muse Glimmer through a hosted provider (Fireworks, then Baseten); at 10:47 SAST the operator removed the outside providers in favour of Muse Spark 1.3 Contributor, and at 11:05 SAST chose Standard Muse Spark instead, because lookalike pages copy real people's names, addresses and numbers and Meta forbids personal data in Contributor input. The rule "Standard model IDs only, never `-contributor`" (`AGENTS.md`) stands unchanged.

> **Current state:** Measured 2026-10-28 15:20 SAST. No screening, dataset or training code exists. The Meta Model API key is in the vault ("IsItLegit Meta Model API"); calls go through AI Gateway `isitlegit` (D00 T01 §6, which has a `custom-meta` route). Fireworks' US$100 credit is loaded (operator, 09:02 SAST); the vault items "IsItLegit Fireworks" (an inference-only service account's key, the Worker's `FIREWORKS_API_KEY`) and "IsItLegit Fireworks Admin" (local scripts only, never a Worker secret) both have filled credentials (checked 11:33 SAST, each lists 29 models with HTTP 200). Glimmer is not served pay-per-token (`accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b` answers 404 and is absent from `/inference/v1/models`), so it runs only on a dedicated deployment. Researched 2026-10-28 from Fireworks' documentation: Glimmer 30B supervised fine-tuning (LoRA) is free through the UI and the Serverless Training API until 2026-11-30; fine-tuned LoRAs deploy only to dedicated deployments, and a single LoRA is best served by live merge; `scale-to-zero-window` defaults to 1h with a 5m minimum, `min-replica-count` 0 enables it; a request to a scaled-to-zero deployment gets an immediate HTTP 503 `DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP` and is not queued, and warm-up is "up to a few minutes" (no number given); billing is per GPU second while replicas are active with no start-up charge, H100 80 GB at US$8.00/h (30 minutes a day is US$4.00); there is no platform setting for a daily runtime cap; deployments with zero minimum replicas are deleted after 7 days without traffic; training data is JSONL in OpenAI chat format with base64 images in `user` messages, 3 to 3 million lines, training context 32,768 tokens by default; whether Glimmer's free SFT accepts images is not documented (§2 proves it). `samples/` (git-ignored except `README.md` and `.gitignore`) holds 543 `.eml` files: 533 from the pilot client's Defender quarantine in its own subfolder (Microsoft verdict in each filename; the subfolder is named only in the git-ignored `samples/NOTES.md`) and the operator's own mail in `samples/gmail/` and `samples/outlook/`, labelled in `samples/manifest.csv`; The pilot client's consent (`samples/pilot-consent.md`) covers the Meta Model API and, since 2026-10-28 15:16 SAST, redacted content in Fireworks training lines. `playwright-core` 1.64.1 is a dev dependency and `test/node/browser.ts` exports `chromiumPath()`. History: on 2026-10-27 the Fireworks account had no credit and Baseten's covered only Muse Spark Open; neither was used.

## Inputs

- Design spec 4.8 (Meta data terms: Standard model IDs only), 4.9 (licences of public corpora), 4.11 (monitor screening), 9 (model map), 11 (budget: Fireworks US$90 in total and US$5 a day; Meta US$120 total and US$15 a day).
- Fireworks AI documentation: free Glimmer training (https://fireworks.ai/blog/train-muse-glimmer-for-free, https://fireworks.ai/pricing), deploying LoRAs (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras), autoscaling (https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling), deployment management (https://docs.fireworks.ai/api-reference/training-sdk/deployment-management), vision fine-tuning data format (https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/fine-tuning-vlm), on-demand billing (https://docs.fireworks.ai/faq-new/deployment-infrastructure/how-does-billing-work-for-on-demand-deployments), the Muse Glimmer 30B model page (https://fireworks.ai/models/fireworks/muse-glimmer-30b).
- `samples/README.md` and the local `samples/pilot-consent.md` (consent terms for the sample mail).
- -> XREF: D08 T01 §2 -- the licence rule for public corpora that §5 applies to page datasets
- -> XREF: D05 T04 §2 -- the daily monitor run that calls §15's `wakeGlimmer` at its start

## Outcome

- A fine-tuned Glimmer model on Fireworks, trained free on a deduplicated, seed-disjoint dataset whose sources, licences and counts are recorded in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` and `docs/glimmer/training.md`.
- A dedicated deployment that scales to zero 5 minutes after its last request, whose measured warm-up is recorded, and whose running time IsItLegit holds to 30 minutes a day on average, one day borrowed ahead (§34).
- `screenPage(screenshot, text, ctx)` returns a structured "brand lure with login or payment form" judgement from the tuned Glimmer, or from Muse Spark when Glimmer cannot answer in time or within its minutes, naming which answered and why, with its cost recorded against the monitor run that asked.
- `docs/frontier-log.md` records the tuned Glimmer's and Spark's accuracy, misses, latency and cost per page on the held-out set, side by side.

**Adjacency:** all=not-applicable (internal screening step for the monitor; results are shown through monitor findings, D05 T04 §5)

**Adjacency rationale:** Screening only feeds the monitor pipeline; the training and deployment scripts are local operator tooling with no product surface.

## 2. Fireworks account check and image probe

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T14:12:00Z

Before any data is built, prove what the account can do: list models, deployments and credit with the admin key, and run the smallest possible free training job with images to learn whether Glimmer's free SFT accepts screenshots. The answer decides whether training lines carry the screenshot or only host, text and form structure. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (checked against the live API, 14:05 UTC):** Fireworks exposes no credit balance: `GET /v1/accounts/rizonetech/credits` answers 404 and the documented calls are credit redemptions (`creditRedemptions`, one, redeemed 2026-10-28 06:47 UTC) and rated spend (`billing/summary`, `billingUsage`), so the check lists those instead of a balance, and the operator's US$100 stays the balance of record. The account ID is `rizonetech` (`GET /v1/accounts`). The Glimmer resource reads `supportsImageInput: true`, `supervisedLoraTunable: true`, `enableVisionEncoderTuning: false` and `trainingContextLength` 131072, so the probe still decides whether image lines train. The probe needs a status and a clean-up mode, which the first draft left to hand calls.

**Blocked 2026-10-28 (14:16 UTC):** Fireworks refuses dataset uploads for account `rizonetech`: `POST /datasets/isitlegit-probe:upload` answers HTTP 403 and `:getUploadEndpoint` HTTP 400, both "dataset uploads are temporarily disabled, please contact Fireworks AI to get access"; the account reads `accountVerification: NOT_VERIFIED`. The operator verifies the account or asks Fireworks to enable uploads, records the answer here, and removes `operator` from Needs; §11's training upload waits on the same switch. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (15:13 UTC, operator: the account is not restricted and uploads work in the UI):** the refusal belongs to the principal, not the account: the key used is the service account `isitlegit-meta-api-admin` (role `admin`), and the operator's own user `rizonetech` uploads normally. The operator either stores an API key of their own user in the vault as "IsItLegit Fireworks Owner" (`credential` field), which `fireworks-check.ts` then uses for dataset uploads only, or uploads `build/glimmer/probe.jsonl` in the UI as dataset `isitlegit-probe`. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (15:26 UTC):** the operator's own key is refused the same way (HTTP 403), and so is the dashboard: Fireworks has dataset uploads closed for the account. The operator asks Fireworks to open them; the Serverless Training API, which streams examples from the client, is checked as the alternative.

**Unblocked 2026-10-28 (16:00 UTC):** the operator stored their own key ("IsItLegit Fireworks Owner", HTTP 200 at 15:20 UTC) and the serverless route needs no upload, so `operator` leaves Needs; managed uploads stay closed until Fireworks reopens them.

## 3. PageScreen contract and training store

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T15:08:00Z

One definition of what the screener sees and answers, shared by training and production, and one local store for every page the dataset is built from. Training on a different prompt than production uses throws away most of the gain, so the request builder lives in `src/` and both sides import it.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Write `src/vision/page-screen.ts`. Done when: the schema, prompt and request builder are tested.
2. Write the store library `scripts/glimmer/store.ts`. Done when: pages are written atomically outside the repository and read back.

- [x] Define `PageAnswer` in `src/vision/page-screen.ts` with zod, strict (unknown keys refused): `brand` (string or null), `has_login_form`, `has_payment_form`, `lure` (booleans), `lure_type` (`credential`, `payment`, `fake_shop`, `impersonation` for a lookalike company, supplier or social page with no form, or `none`; `none` exactly when `lure` is false) and `confidence` (0 to 1); and `PageScreen` as a `PageAnswer` plus `screener` (`glimmer` or `spark`), made only by `screened(answer, screener)`, never parsed from model output. Done when: `test/vision/page-screen.test.ts` accepts a valid answer, refuses each missing or mistyped field, a `lure_type` that contradicts `lure` and an answer carrying `screener`, and shows `screened()` sets the caller's screener. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** `lure_type` added (operator question 2026-10-28 16:12 SAST: fake shops, social networks and corporate sites too; widened 16:15 SAST); the model's answer and the caller's result split, because a training answer carries no `screener` (plan review finding 2).
- [x] Write `SCREENER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `src/vision/page-screen.ts`: the job (is this page a fake: a credential or payment lure, a fake shop, or a lookalike company, supplier or social page, and which of those), D03 T01 §6's warning sentence about untrusted blocks, each `PageAnswer` field with its constraint, and the instruction to answer with that JSON alone, without `screener`. Done when: the test finds the warning sentence, the four lure kinds, each field and the JSON-only rule, fails on a copy with any one removed, and pins the prompt's hash so any change shows up in review. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 4):** a pinned hash alone passed an incomplete prompt.
- [x] Write `buildScreenRequest({ image, host, text, mode, nonce })` returning the chat `messages` (system prompt; `user` with the host, the visible text in an untrusted block and, when `mode` is `image`, the screenshot as an `image_url` part), truncating text to 6,000 characters at a whole character. The block uses D03 T01 §6's format (`<<UNTRUSTED:page-text:<nonce>>>` to `<<END:<nonce>>>`, any `<<UNTRUSTED` or `<<END` in the text removed first, and its warning sentence in the system prompt), with the 128-bit nonce passed in (random when omitted) so the same inputs build the same request. Done when: `test/vision/page-screen.test.ts` shows `text-only` mode carries no image part, long text is cut at a whole character, hostile text containing `<<END:x>>` stays inside the block, and two calls with one nonce are equal. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** D03 T01 §6's `untrusted()` (Phase 1) is not built and a Phase 0 section cannot wait on it, so the block is built here in its format, and §1 moves the production call onto `untrusted()` once it ships; a fixed nonce is what lets §1 prove the production request equals the training one.
- [x] Write `buildTrainingLine(request, answer)` returning one Fireworks JSONL line: every message of the request unchanged, then the `assistant` message holding the `PageAnswer` JSON, with the screenshot as a `data:image/jpeg;base64,` URI in image mode. Done when: the test round-trips an image-mode line through `JSON.parse`, decodes its image back to the original JPEG bytes, finds the request's messages unchanged before the answer and the answer valid against `PageAnswer`, and checks a text-only line separately. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/fine-tuning-models#vision-training. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 3):** JSON parsing alone passed a line with no usable image or an altered request; that the production request matches is still §1's to prove.
- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/store.ts` resolving the store at `GLIMMER_DATA_DIR` (default `~/isitlegit-data/glimmer`) and refusing any path inside this repository or any other git work tree, as `scripts/astra-collect.py` does. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` shows a repository path and a path in another checkout are refused. Cheaper substitute: a git-ignored folder in the repository, one `git add -f` from a public leak. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** any git work tree, matching the collector (§4).
- [x] Write `putPage(source, page)` storing `pages/<id>/screenshot.jpg` (JPEG only, at most 1280 px wide and 2000 px tall, read from its header, so a larger or non-JPEG screenshot is refused rather than resized), `text.txt` and `meta.json` (`source`, `origin`, `licence`, `host` (the candidate host, or null for a page with none, such as a mail attachment), `template` (the synthetic template's name, or null), `south_africa` (boolean), `labels`, `label_source` (for each label field, one of `ground-truth`, `dom`, `construction`, `spark`, `astra+claude` or `claude`), `sha256`, `redacted`), where `<id>` is the source and the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the screenshot and text, built in a temporary folder and renamed into place, and returning the existing id when the same page is stored again. Done when: the test writes a page, reopens the store and reads every file and field back equal through `manifest()`, stores the same page twice as one, refuses an oversized and a PNG screenshot, and an interrupted write leaves no partial page. Cheaper substitute: writing files in place. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** the id, the JPEG rule and the repeat rule were unstated. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 1):** host, template, South African marker and per-field label source added; §3's request builder needs the host and §10 needs the rest for its splits and its evaluation rule.
- [x] Write `manifest()` listing every stored page with source, labels and hashes, and `counts()` by source and label. Done when: the test's three stored pages appear with correct counts.
- [x] Write `estimateTokens(line, mode)` for both training modes (text at 3 characters a token; `image` adds one token per 14 by 14 pixel patch of the screenshot, Glimmer's ViT-G/14 encoder, a deliberate overestimate until §2's probe or §11's job reports real counts; `text-only` adds none) and refuse lines above 30,000 tokens (under Fireworks' 32,768 training context). Done when: the test refuses an oversized line in each mode. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** was the mode recorded in §2, which made §3 and every data section behind it wait on the Fireworks upload block (D03 T04 §2, `operator`); §10 applies the recorded mode instead and now depends on §2. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/fine-tuning-vlm
- [x] State `brand`'s 200-character limit in the prompt so a model following it never writes an answer the schema refuses (completion review, gap 1). Done when: the prompt test requires the limit and the hash is pinned again.
- [x] Validate the host as a lowercase DNS name or null in `buildScreenRequest` (`HOST`) and in the store's `meta.json`, since it sits outside the untrusted block (completion review, gap 2). Done when: both tests refuse a newline, a delimiter and an upper-case host.
- [x] Strip delimiters in one pass (`stripDelimiters`) with the same result as removing them until none is left (completion review, gap 3). Done when: the test matches the old fixpoint on 500 generated strings and builds the crafted 100,000-character page in under 250 ms (about 25 ms measured, 2,852 ms before).
- [x] Refuse a JPEG unless every segment is complete, a frame precedes the scan and the end marker closes it, and prove the training line with a real JPEG (`test/fixtures/glimmer/page.jpg`, encoded and decoded by Pillow in `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fixtures.py`) (completion review, gap 4). Done when: every cut of a JPEG is refused and the real fixture reaches a training line byte for byte.
- [x] Flush each page's files and folders to disk, remove a dead writer's temporary or half-removed folder on open, name damaged pages in `verify()`, and replace a damaged page on a repeated `putPage` (completion review, gap 5). Done when: the store tests cover a dead and a live writer's folder, a damaged screenshot named and then replaced.
- [x] Write `removeSource(source)` taking a source's pages out whole, for §5's rejected sources (completion review, gap 6, built here rather than filed). Done when: the test removes one source and keeps another that shares its prefix.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: PageScreen contract and training store`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/vision/page-screen.test.ts test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` passes and fails if the store accepts a path inside the repository or the training line differs from the production request.

The operator chose Astra (GPT-6 Astra through the Codex CLI) to collect public datasets (2026-10-28 15:16 SAST). Claude writes every script and verifies everything Astra collects; Astra keeps reviewing Claude's code, never its own data.

**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Record the role change in `AGENTS.md`. Done when: the Astra paragraph names collection and its limits.
2. Write the brief and `scripts/astra-collect.py`. Done when: a dry run prints the exact Codex command rooted outside the repository.

Done when: `tests/scripts/test_astra_collect.py` finds in the Astra paragraph each rule (collection jobs, `workspace-write` rooted at the data store, never in the repository, Claude writes the brief and every script and verifies the data, Astra never reviews data it collected, label judging at `xhigh` in `read-only`, sample pages only when redacted) and in `CONTRIBUTIONS.md` Astra's dataset collection and label judging, and fails on a copy with any one removed. Cheaper substitute: an unrecorded exception. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 4):** was a single `grep -c 'collection jobs'`, which proves none of the limits.
- [x] Write the brief `docs/glimmer/astra-collection-brief.md`: the goal (phishing, fake-shop, scam-site and benign web pages with screenshots, HTML or visible text, and brand or kind labels), candidate sources to research (Phishpedia, PhishIntention, VisualPhishNet, PILWD-134K, fake-shop and scam-website corpora, and any others found), the licence rule (accept only licences allowing use in a commercial product's model training; research-only, non-commercial or unclear is recorded and not downloaded; spec 4.9), safety rules (download only from the dataset's official page; never open a live phishing URL; never execute files from a download), the output layout (`raw/<source>/` plus `raw/<source>/COLLECTED.md` with URL, licence name, licence quote and link, file list with SHA-256, item count, date), the recovery rules (each download goes to `raw/<source>/.partial/` and moves into place only after its SHA-256 is computed; `COLLECTED.md` is written last, through a temporary file and rename, only when every listed file is in place; a rerun skips a source whose `COLLECTED.md` hashes still match, and deletes `.partial/` and redoes any source without one), and the stop conditions (at most 3 attempts a file, 5 GB a source, 20 GB in all, 30 candidate sources, and 4 hours; reaching one ends the run with a report of what is complete and what is not). Done when: `test/node/astra-brief.test.ts` finds each heading with its substance (the research-only, non-commercial and unclear exclusions, each safety prohibition, each `COLLECTED.md` field, the `.partial/` rule and each numeric stop limit), and fails on a copy of the brief with any one removed. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review findings 3 and 4):** the stop conditions were unnamed, interrupted downloads had no recovery rule, and a heading-only test passed an empty brief.

## 5. Public phishing-page datasets by licence

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T16:40:00Z

Astra researches and downloads public phishing-page datasets under the brief (§4); Claude re-checks every licence at its source, converts the accepted sets into the store and spot-checks their labels. The licence rule is the one D08 T01 §2 applies to mail corpora: anything unclear is not used.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Run the collection. Done when: each candidate has a `COLLECTED.md` or a recorded rejection.
2. Verify and convert. Done when: `docs/glimmer/sources.md` lists every candidate and accepted sets are in the store.

- [x] Run `python3 scripts/astra-collect.py` in the background and read Astra's report (`<store>/raw/ASTRA-REPORT.md`) and `RUN-STATUS.json`. Done when: every candidate source in the brief has `raw/<source>/COLLECTED.md` or a rejection line with its reason.
- [x] Re-read each accepted source's licence at its own URL and check every downloaded file's SHA-256 against `COLLECTED.md`. Done when: each source is confirmed or rejected by Claude, and a mismatch removes the source. Cheaper substitute: trusting Astra's licence summary.
- [x] Record every candidate in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` (public facts only, one row each: `| Source | Dataset | Licence | Licence link | Decision | Reason | Label errors |`), checked by `reconcile()` in `scripts/glimmer/sources.ts`. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` finds a decision and licence link on each row, and for each accepted source its expected, imported, duplicate and excluded counts from `IMPORT.json`. Checked 2026-10-29 against the real store (121,135 pages, every import complete): `node scripts/glimmer/sources.ts check` -> `sources: reconciled`, after 7 Days Later's row was brought up to date (accepted, with its counts) and its attribution added to `NOTICE.md`.
- [x] List each accepted source in `NOTICE.md`'s third-party table with its licence, its link and what it is used for (training Loupe, not shipped in the Worker). Done when: `test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` fails when an accepted source in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` is missing from `NOTICE.md`. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 5):** the project's register of third-party data is `NOTICE.md` (`eval/DATASETS.md` comes with the eval harness).
- [x] Write `extract()` in `scripts/glimmer/extract_archive.py` unpacking a downloaded archive (zip or tar, gzip or not) into a fresh folder with every entry confined to it (absolute paths, `..` and links refused) and the expanded size held to 5 GB a source, stopping cleanly on a write failure (a full disk included) with the folder removed and the source's `IMPORT.json` reading `incomplete` with the reason (Astra never unpacks, §4 completion review C3). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_extract_archive.py` extracts a two-file zip and tar.gz and reads every byte back, refuses a `../` entry, an absolute path, a symbolic link and an archive that expands past the limit, and turns an injected write failure into no folder and an `incomplete` `IMPORT.json`. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 4):** a refuse-everything extractor passed the earlier tests, and the disk failure was untested. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (at build):** Python's standard `zipfile` and `tarfile` own the formats, so the extractor is Python with no new dependency, each entry checked before a byte is written.
- [x] Write `normaliseScreenshot()` in `scripts/glimmer/source-pages.ts` re-encoding any source image with sharp to a JPEG at most 1280 px wide (resized) and 2000 px tall (cropped from the top), since public sets hold PNGs and full-page captures the store refuses as they are. Done when: the test turns a 2560 by 6000 PNG into a decodable 1280 by 2000 JPEG and refuses a file sharp cannot decode. Tested in `test/node/glimmer-source-pages.test.ts`.
- [x] Write `openTextReader()` in `scripts/glimmer/source-pages.ts`, whose `visibleText()` takes a page's visible text from its HTML through inert Chromium (no JavaScript, every request refused, `innerText`) (scripts, styles, comments, `hidden` and `display:none` or `visibility:hidden` elements dropped, entities decoded, whitespace collapsed) for sources that ship HTML rather than text. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-source-pages.test.ts`'s fixture keeps the heading and the form labels, drops a script, a style, a comment, a `hidden` element and a `display:none` block, decodes `&amp;` and `&#x27;`, and collapses runs of whitespace. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 4):** each promised removal is now tested. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (at build):** Chromium owns rendering, so it decides what is visible rather than a hand-written stripper; `chromiumPath()` moved to `scripts/lib/chromium.ts`, re-exported by `test/node/browser.ts`.
- [x] Write one converter per accepted source, `scripts/glimmer/import-<source>.ts`, storing each item through `await putPage` with `origin: public`, its licence, the page's host (from the dataset's URL, or null), `template: null`, `south_africa` from the host's `.za` suffix or the dataset's country field, its source labels (`lure`, `brand`, and `lure_type` when the set gives one) with `label_source` `ground-truth` for each, and `redacted: false`. Each converter writes `raw/<source>/IMPORT.json` (temp-then-rename) with the items expected, imported, already stored (duplicates) and excluded, each exclusion with its item and reason (an image that will not decode, a label outside `PageAnswer`, a missing file); a source with more than 5 percent excluded, or one stopped by a write failure, reads `incomplete` and is not used until a rerun completes it; a page that loads but renders under 20 characters of text is listed apart as `filtered` and does not count toward the 5 percent (**Corrected 2026-10-28:** PhreshPhish's first import had 466 such pages, 6.9 percent, which draw everything with scripts the inert renderer never runs; no rerun can change what the dataset holds, so counting them as failures would have made the source unusable); a rerun stores only what is missing (`putPage` keeps one copy) and leaves other sources untouched. Done when: the converter's test converts a two-item fixture (one PNG and one oversized), reopens the store and reads back each page's screenshot and text paired, host, labels and `label_source`; a failure injected after the first page leaves `incomplete`, and a rerun finishes with no duplicate and another source unchanged. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review findings 2 and 3):** counts alone could not catch a wrong mapping, and a partial import had no defined outcome. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** `putPage` is async and its page now carries host, template, the South African marker and per-field label sources (§3).
- [x] Spot-check 50 random pages per source by viewing their screenshots against their labels. Done when: the label error rate per source is recorded in `docs/glimmer/training.md` under `Dataset`. Measured 2026-10-28: ScamFerret 4 percent at most, PhreshPhish 6 percent; neither is above the line.
- [x] Mark a source with more than 10 percent label errors as unverified: its pages' `lure` and `lure_type` `label_source` become `unverified` (a value added to the store's `LABEL_SOURCES`), which keeps them out of evaluation (§10 takes only ground truth, Claude's decision or Astra and Claude's agreement) and sends each to §9's independent adjudication by Astra and Claude, whose agreement replaces the label and whose disagreement marks the page `ambiguous`. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` stores and reads back `unverified`, and `test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` fails when a source recorded above 10 percent errors still has a page whose `lure` reads `ground-truth`. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 1):** was relabelling by §9's teacher, which §9 forbids to set `lure`.
- [x] If no source is acceptable, record that in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` and raise §8's synthetic lure target and its `Done when:` count from 1,500 to 3,000. Done when: either imports exist, or the decision is recorded and `test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` fails while §8 still reads 1,500 in either place. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 6):** a recorded decision alone left §8 unchanged.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: public phishing-page datasets chosen by licence`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts test/node/glimmer-source-pages.test.ts`, `python3 -m unittest tests.scripts.test_extract_archive` and each converter's test pass, and `node scripts/glimmer/sources.ts check` prints `sources: reconciled` against the real store; `glimmer-sources.test.ts` reconciles both ways, failing when an accepted source in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` has no complete `IMPORT.json` or a stored count that differs from it, when a rejected source has any page in the store, when a store source has no row, or when the manifest is empty while a source is accepted.

## 6. Benign real pages rendered locally

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T16:58:00Z

The screener must tell lures from the real pages they copy, so the training set needs many real login, checkout and home pages, rendered by our own browser from known-good URLs only. Form flags come from the page's DOM, not a model.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Write the URL lists. Done when: the Majestic slice and the South African list exist.
2. Write and run `scripts/glimmer/render.ts`. Done when: benign pages are in the store with DOM form flags.

- [x] Download the Majestic Million (CC BY 3.0, https://majestic.com/reports/majestic-million) and keep, in the store as `raw/majestic/top.csv`, its top 5,000 domains plus its 600 highest-ranked `.za` domains, recording the licence, the download date and the attribution under `docs/glimmer/sources.md` and in `NOTICE.md`. A list is used only when its licence is on the commercial-use allowlist in `render.ts` (CC0, CC BY 3.0 or 4.0, MIT, Apache 2.0, or our own). Done when: the file has the rows, `test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts` refuses a list recorded under CC BY-NC or with no licence, and `glimmer-sources.test.ts` finds the NOTICE.md entry. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 1):** was the Tranco list, which merges a CC BY-NC 4.0 source (Cloudflare Radar) and so fails the licence rule (design spec 4.9). The captured pages themselves are our own renders of public pages, used only to train Loupe and never published or redistributed; that basis is recorded under `Decisions` in `docs/glimmer/training.md`, and changing it means dropping the benign renders. Measured 2026-10-28: 1,000,000 domains downloaded; `top.csv` keeps 5,597 (the top 5,000 and the 597 highest-ranked `.za` domains not among them); the record is a paragraph above the table in `docs/glimmer/sources.md`, since the table's rows are datasets whose imports it reconciles.
- [x] Write `eval/glimmer/benign-sa-urls.txt`: at least 150 login, checkout and account pages of South African brands lures copy (banks, SARS eFiling, telcos, retailers, couriers, municipalities, Microsoft 365 and Google sign-in), plus at least 50 real shop product and checkout pages, 50 company about and contact pages (including suppliers' banking-details pages where public), and 30 public social network profile or page views, each line tagged with its kind. Done when: `test/node/benign-urls.test.ts` counts at least 150 login, checkout or account URLs and the three new kinds at their minimums, all `https`, none duplicated. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** the shop, company and social kinds added (operator question 2026-10-28 16:12 SAST: fake shops, social networks and corporate sites too; widened 16:15 SAST). Written 2026-10-28: 195 sign-in, checkout and account URLs, 63 shop, 64 company and 44 social, each with its brand. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (completion review, gap 3):** most of those were home pages under a kind's name. Each root-listed site's home page is now tagged `home` (a benign South African page counted toward no minimum), and its sign-in, product, cart, about or contact page was found from its own links or common paths, each answering 200 on the same site; further sign-in, account and shop pages came the same way from Majestic's `.za` domains. The list now holds 187 sign-in, checkout and account URLs, 64 shop, 52 company and 44 social; `benign-urls.test.ts` refuses a shop, company or checkout URL at a site's root and a root sign-in URL on a site's main host, and `capturedKind()` stores a capture under its kind only when the page shows it.
- [x] Write `renderPage()` in `scripts/glimmer/render.ts` with `playwright-core` and `chromiumPath()` (`scripts/lib/chromium.ts`): a fresh context per page, 1280 by 800 viewport, downloads, permissions, pop-ups and service workers refused, no stored credentials, a 20-second limit, the full-page screenshot as a JPEG cut to 2000 px through `normaliseScreenshot()` (§5), and the visible text (`innerText`). Requests go only to public addresses: a host that is or resolves to a loopback, private, link-local or unique-local address is refused at every request and redirect. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts` renders a local fixture page with both parts (the test server allowed by name), and proves that a download writes nothing, a pop-up is closed, a service worker is not registered, the context is closed after a failure, the whole render stops at 20 seconds, and a redirect to `127.0.0.1` or `10.0.0.1` is refused. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 4):** each isolation promise is now tested, and the local-network boundary is named. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** `chromiumPath()` moved to `scripts/lib/chromium.ts` (§5), and the context's limits are named. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation):** Playwright calls a route only for a redirect chain's first URL, so every request is fetched by the route one hop at a time (`route.fetch` with no redirects) and each `Location` is checked before the browser follows it; WebSockets are refused too. The test proves it with a second live local server that must receive no request, and with an allowed redirect that is still followed.
- [x] Set `has_login_form` from any visible sign-in field, in the page or in a frame (a password input, or a username or email input: `type=email`, `autocomplete` `username` or `email`, or a name, id or label reading user, email, login or sign-in), and `has_payment_form` from visible card fields (`autocomplete` `cc-number`, `cc-exp` or `cc-csc`) or a payment-provider frame, in `render.ts`; hidden and `type=hidden` fields never count. Done when: the test's username-only, email-only, password, hidden-field-only, card and payment-frame fixtures get the right flags. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 2):** was password inputs alone, which disagreed with the prompt's definition of a login form (`src/vision/page-screen.ts`) and would have trained identifier-first sign-ins as having no login form.
- [x] Accept a capture only when it is usable: HTTP status 2xx, the final page on the listed site's registrable domain (or a domain recorded for it in the list), at least 50 characters of visible text after the page settles (load, then up to 3 seconds without network), and none of the challenge or wall signs (`Just a moment`, `Verify you are human`, `Access denied`, a CAPTCHA frame, a cookie wall covering the page); anything else is excluded with its reason, never stored as benign. Done when: the test excludes an HTTP 404, a cross-site redirect, a loading shell, a challenge page and a consent wall, and keeps a real page. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review finding 3):** popularity is not verification, and a render can capture a page that is not the site.
- [x] Write the batch run in `render.ts`: four pages at a time, each rendered page stored through `await putPage` with `origin: benign`, `licence` naming its list, `host` (the final URL's lowercase host), `template: null`, `south_africa` (a `.za` host or the South African list), labels `lure: false`, `lure_type: none`, the DOM form flags and, from the South African list, `brand`, with `label_source` `ground-truth` for `lure`, `lure_type` and `brand` and `dom` for the form flags; `url` and `kind` (the list's tag: `home`, `login`, `checkout`, `account`, `shop`, `company` or `social`, two optional fields added to the store's `meta.json`); each URL's outcome in `raw/benign/progress.jsonl` as `started` before the render and `stored` (with its page id), `excluded` (with the reason) or `retry` (a timeout or a 5xx, tried again on a rerun, at most three times) after it, through an append that a rerun repairs (a truncated last line dropped, the file rewritten temp-then-rename). A URL `started` with no outcome is looked up in the store by its `url` before it is rendered again, so a stop between storing and recording never makes a second page. Done when: the test renders three local fixture URLs, stores two, excludes one, finds a stop injected between `putPage` and the progress write completed on rerun with one page, repairs a truncated journal, and retries a 503 once. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** `putPage` is async and needs §3's page fields; about 5,000 live renders need concurrency and a resumable run (plan review finding 5: a stop between store and journal, a truncated journal and retryable failures each have a rule). **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation):** some Chromium builds fail the navigation of an empty 5xx (`net::ERR_HTTP_RESPONSE_CODE_FAILURE`) instead of answering it (CI run 40749362466); the guard records each page's main-frame status and a failed navigation after an error status is reported as `HttpStatus`, a 5xx retried and a 4xx excluded.
- [x] Render the homepage of each listed domain and every South African URL with the batch run in the background, replacing each excluded South African URL by another of its kind until the kind's minimum of usable captures holds. Done when: `benignCoverage()` in `render.ts`, reading the store's manifest scoped to `origin: benign`, reports at least 3,000 pages, at least 300 of them South African, and at least 120 `login`, `checkout` or `account`, 40 `shop`, 40 `company` and 20 `social` captures, each read back with its screenshot and text paired and its `brand`, `south_africa` and `label_source` intact; its test fails when pages of another origin labelled benign are what reach the totals. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (plan review findings 6 and 7):** `counts()` has no origin, region or kind breakdown, and URL-list minimums did not guarantee captures of each kind. **Done 2026-10-29:** `render.ts coverage` at 13:09 SAST read 3,465 benign pages, 443 South African, 121 sign-in, checkout or account, 42 shop, 40 company and 36 social, with no problems; the South African list grew by 52 sign-in and account pages checked to answer on their own sites (c21cfeb, b42d72e, aea0a6c and earlier), a reader fault that excluded 31 good pages was fixed and those pages reopened (58594ae), and 13 stored bot walls were taken out (36f15c3). The Majestic batch keeps rendering the rest of its 20,576 domains for §18 and 1.1; §6's minimums do not wait on it.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: benign real pages rendered locally`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts test/node/benign-urls.test.ts` passes and fails if a username-only, password or card field is missed, an unusable or cross-site capture is stored, a redirect reaches a private address, a rerun stores a page twice, or the benign coverage counts pages of another origin; `node scripts/glimmer/render.ts --coverage` prints the coverage against the real store.

## 7. Redacted sample mail converted to pages

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T23:36Z

**Corrected 2026-10-29:** this section's code was written on 2026-10-28 (6b04f54, 9ff7619) beside §5's importers, before a `Started:` line or a plan review existed, so the plan review runs now against the section as written and every gap it names is fixed in code before the stamp. §6 (its prerequisite) has finished its reviews and waits only on its live render, so this review runs ahead of §6's stamp to keep §9 to §11 inside the 2026-11-01 12:00 SAST training cutoff; §7 stamps only after §6.

The 543 sample emails carry real South African lures: credential-harvest HTML attachments that are login pages in themselves, and brand-styled bodies. Under the consent extension recorded 2026-10-28 15:16 SAST, redacted content may go into Fireworks training lines; unredacted content goes only to the Meta Model API. Nothing from `samples/` reaches the repository.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Inventory and redact. Done when: the redactor's tests pass on synthetic fixtures.
2. Render attachments and bodies inertly. Done when: sample-derived pages are in the store, marked redacted, and the leak test passes.

- [x] Extend the pilot-client rule in `AGENTS.md` (the "Never send real client mail to the Meta Model API" bullet): redacted content may also go into Fireworks training lines for this screener (operator, 2026-10-28 15:16 SAST) and to GPT-6 Astra through the Codex CLI for this work (operator, 2026-10-28 15:34 SAST), unredacted content still goes only to the Meta Model API, and nothing enters a tracked file. Done when: `grep -c 'Fireworks training lines' AGENTS.md` prints `1`. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review gap 11):** a phrase count proves nothing about the rule; `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` now asserts the bullet's permission (redacted, Fireworks training lines for Loupe) and its restrictions (unredacted only to the Meta Model API, never in a tracked file, kept under the git-ignored `samples/`). Cheaper substitute: relying on the git-ignored consent file alone while `AGENTS.md` still forbids the use. Done 2026-10-28 on main (c930f9a, at the lane's request, since `AGENTS.md` is main's under the lane rules), merged into the lane at b8bbf8e; the Astra part is moot since the operator removed the judges (§9).
- [x] Confirm `samples/pilot-consent.md` has the "Extension: Fireworks training (2026-10-28 15:16 SAST)" section before reading any sample. Done when: `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts` refuses to run without it and its test proves the refusal.
- [x] Write the inventory step of `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts`: per file, its label (`samples/manifest.csv`, or the Microsoft verdict in a quarantine filename), its HTML attachments and whether it has an HTML body. Done when: it prints counts only (no names, subjects or addresses) and the counts sum to 543. Measured 2026-10-28: 543 messages, 534 lures (533 Microsoft phishing verdicts, 1 operator-labelled attack), 9 operator-labelled genuine, 19 HTML attachments and 510 HTML bodies.
- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` replacing email addresses, person names (from the headers and `samples/client-names.txt`), phone numbers, South African ID numbers, bank account and card numbers with fixed placeholders in text and HTML before rendering. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-redact.test.ts` redacts each kind in a synthetic fixture and leaves brand names and links' host names intact. Cheaper substitute: redacting only the visible text while the screenshot still shows the originals.
- [x] Render each redacted HTML attachment with JavaScript and network off (inert) through `openTextReader().renderHtml` in `scripts/glimmer/source-pages.ts` (**Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation):** was `render.ts`, which renders live URLs with scripts on; the inert renderer is §5's), each body with its `cid:` images put back as data URIs so a brand's logo survives the network-free render, storing it with `origin: samples`, `redacted: true` and the label from the inventory. Done when: `counts()` shows the attachment pages.
- [x] Render each redacted HTML body the same way. Done when: `counts()` shows the body pages and `Spam` or `Bulk` verdicts are stored with `lure` unset for §9 to decide. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review gap 6):** §9's judges were removed, so no step decides those labels; `Spam`, `Bulk`, another verdict and no label are now counted exclusions (below).
- [x] Write the leak check `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` that scans every `origin: samples` page's text for the pilot client's domain, any address on a recipient line, and every name in `samples/client-names.txt`. Done when: it finds none, and fails on a fixture page that keeps one. Measured 2026-10-28 19:20 UTC: `node scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts leak-check` reads `clean (270 pages)`: 4 attachment and 266 body pages stored, 228 bodies identical to another, 30 filtered (script-only pages that render blank without JavaScript) and 1 refused because a client detail survived redaction. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation):** the first import found 4 pages holding the client's domains or a client name inside an address split by tags; the client's domains are now redacted too, and every rendered page is checked against the client's names, addresses and domains before it is stored, a hit refusing the page.
- [x] Replace every picture before the render with `stripImages()` in `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` (an `<img>` becomes a grey box of its size; other embedded media, CSS images and `background` attributes go) and refuse any HTML `hasImages()` still finds, so no signature image or personalised attachment reaches a screenshot (plan review gap 1). Done when: the end-to-end test in `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` finds no picture in anything rendered and the logo became a blank box. Decision: privacy over logo cues; keeping logos needs an OCR and named-entity pass over each image, which this host lacks (reversal: add that pass and drop the box rule). Cheaper substitute: checking only the rendered text while the screenshot still shows an inline image.
- [x] Read character references and hidden characters as the render shows them (`decodeTextEntities()`), redact bracketed area codes (`(011) 555 1234`) and capitalised two-letter names, require `samples/client-names.txt`, and refuse a rendered page whose text still shows any recipient name piece, address, client domain, phone or long number (`renderRedacted()`, `personalDetail()`) (plan review gap 2). Done when: `test/node/glimmer-redact.test.ts` redacts `Th&#97;ndi`, a soft-hyphenated name, `Jo` and `(011) 555 1234`, and the end-to-end test refuses a page whose phone only the render joined up, by kind and never by the number.
- [x] Remove every earlier sample page when the redaction's key (`redactionKey()`: `redact.ts`, `import-samples.ts`, the client names, the manifest) differs from `raw/samples/REDACTION.json`, and write the key only when every message is accounted for and both imports are complete (plan review gap 3). Done when: the end-to-end test changes the client names, sees every earlier page removed, keeps the old key through a rebuild that fails part-way, and records the new key after a complete one.
- [x] Account for every message in `raw/samples/INVENTORY.json` (written temp-then-rename): each part a page item, an `unsupported <type>` part, or `no body`; each message an outcome, an exclusion or a failure; plain-text bodies laid out and rendered as pages (plan review gap 4). Done when: the end-to-end test's nine messages are each accounted for and `leakCheck()` reports none unaccounted.
- [x] Decode each HTML attachment's bytes by its BOM or declared charset, else as UTF-8 when valid, else Windows-1252 (`decodeHtml()` in `scripts/glimmer/source-pages.ts`), and hand the parser the message's raw bytes (plan review gap 5). Done when: the end-to-end test's undeclared Windows-1252 attachment reads `Café` with its encoded name redacted.
- [x] Give `Spam`, `Bulk`, an unknown verdict and an unlabelled message a counted exclusion (`labelOf()`), and refuse a `manifest.csv` without `file` and `label` columns, with a label other than `attack` or `genuine`, or naming no message (`readManifest()`) (plan review gap 6). Done when: the importer's tests prove each exclusion reason and both manifest refusals.
- [x] Reconcile identical pages' labels after each import (`reconcile()`): a page keeps the strongest label its messages agree on (ground truth over unverified) and no `lure` label when they disagree, whatever order they were stored in (plan review gap 7). Done when: the end-to-end test's identical `Phish` and `HighConfPhish` pages read back ground truth, its identical genuine and phishing pages read back with no `lure`, and the test fails with `reconcile()` removed.
- [x] Read each message bounded (`readMessages()`: over 25 MB, unreadable or unparsable within 30 seconds is a failure by its opaque id) and exit 1 while any message failed or either import is incomplete (`runImport()`) (plan review gap 8). Done when: the end-to-end test's unreadable message is a recorded failure, the run returns 1 and records no key.

## 8. Synthetic South African lures

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T15:21Z

Public sets are mostly foreign brands. Synthetic pages fill the South African gap, seeded by the lure patterns the sample mail shows (brand, lure type, call to action), with labels known by construction. Every page is inert: no script, no form action, no external resource, no copied logo file.

**Removed 2026-10-28 (operator, 21:41 SAST, accepting the recommendation):** Claude will not generate realistic lure pages that impersonate real South African brands, so no synthetic lure is made. The redacted pilot-client pages (§7) are the South African lures instead: Microsoft's high-confidence phishing verdict counts as ground truth (155 pages after duplicates), a plain phishing verdict stays `unverified` (105), and with the South African benign renders (§6) they give evaluation its South African slice.

2. Generate and render. Done when: synthetic lures and look-alike benign pages are in the store. **Removed 2026-10-28** with the section. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review):** done instead when `node scripts/glimmer/sources.ts check` refuses any stored page with `origin: synthetic` and any generator file or raw folder, tracked or not.

## 9. Spark teacher labels and human check

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T11:58Z

Public labels say lure or not; the screener also answers brand and form flags. Muse Spark fills the missing fields as a teacher, every disagreement with a source label goes to a review queue that Astra at `xhigh` and Claude decide independently (a label two model families agree on, or no label), and both check the same random sample before anything trains (operator, 2026-10-28 15:31 SAST). Sample-derived pages go to Astra only when redacted (consent extended 2026-10-28 15:34 SAST). The operator accepted the terms risk of Astra's and Claude's decisions becoming training labels (2026-10-28 15:36 SAST: OpenAI forbids using output to develop models that compete with OpenAI, Anthropic's commercial terms forbid training competing AI models; a narrow phishing-page screener is not judged to compete).

**Corrected 2026-10-29:** no longer waits on §8. The operator removed §8 on 2026-10-28 (21:41 SAST; `docs/glimmer/training.md`): no synthetic lure is made, and §7's redacted pilot-client pages are the South African lures instead, so a dependency on §8 could never be met and held §9, and with it the whole training path, closed for good. §8's section stays as the record of that removal.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s9.md`):** a retried Spark attempt escaped the US$20 check and the estimate assumed 400 output tokens where 600 are allowed (gap 1); a stop could pay twice or lose a disagreement (gap 2); a page could end with no outcome (gap 3); a failed or absent check left a page evaluation-eligible (gap 4); stored files were used unverified (gap 5); rule brands split one brand across groups (gap 6); no receipt recorded the run for §10 (gap 7); the ledger date was UTC, not the guard's SAST day (gap 8). The items below carry each; §10's builder reads the teacher's states (`D03 T04 §10`).

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** meta-api
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Label with the teacher. Done when: every stored page has a complete `PageScreen` label or a queue entry.
2. Settle the queue. Done when: every queued page is left out of training and evaluation (the judges were removed by the operator, 2026-10-28 21:32 SAST).
3. Check a sample. Done when: removed by the operator (2026-10-28 21:32 SAST); the §5 spot checks' rates stand.

Done when: `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` derives each type, keeps a source's `fake_shop`, and refuses an invalid record. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** no phishing source gives a lure type, and a page without one cannot become a training line; Spark's US$20 cannot fill it for some 100,000 lures.
- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/teacher-label.ts` sending each page with an incomplete label to `muse-spark-1.3` through `metaResponses` in `src/meta/client.ts` (D03 T01 §1) with `buildScreenRequest` and `SCREENER_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, filling only `brand`, `has_login_form` and `has_payment_form` when missing, each call reserved and settled in the shared Meta ledger (`metaLedger()`, `reserveSpend()`, `settleSpend()` and the run lock in `scripts/meta-smoke.ts`, `run` set to `glimmer-teacher-<run id>`) under a US$20 check over the ledger's `glimmer-` entries, the rule §8 set before it was removed (a test with a temporary `ISITLEGIT_LEDGER_DIR` refuses the call that would pass it) (**Corrected 2026-10-28 (operator, parallel-lane rules, main commit 6798409):** was the store's `spend.json`); `lure` always comes from ground truth (the source set, the known-good URL list, construction, or the sample's Microsoft verdict confirmed by Claude), never from Spark, because a student trained on its teacher's answers cannot beat the teacher (operator goal, 2026-10-28 15:29 SAST: Glimmer must beat Spark). Done when: its stubbed test fills a page's missing `brand`, keeps its source `lure`, and refuses to write a `lure` Spark proposed. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (implementation):** the free rules (`label-rules.ts`) now fill every brand and lure type, so Spark checks rather than fills: within the US$20 it answers the screener's own request for the pages that can enter evaluation (a trusted `lure`), South African first, recording each checked page so a rerun never pays twice; a field still missing is filled as `spark`, which evaluation never trusts. `test/node/glimmer-teacher.test.ts` fills a missing brand, keeps the source's `lure`, settles each call in a temporary ledger and refuses the call that would pass US$20 (it fails with the cap removed).

## 10. Deduplicated seed-disjoint training set

> **Started:** 2026-10-30T07:56Z

The store becomes two files: training and held-out evaluation. Duplicates go, no brand dominates, and the held-out set shares no page, near-duplicate or brand group with training, so the numbers in §13 mean something.

## 11. Serverless LoRA training of Loupe

Train the LoRA on Fireworks while the free offer lasts (until 2026-11-30), with the operator's own key in a local script only. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** was a managed SFT job on an uploaded dataset; Fireworks has closed dataset uploads platform-wide with no date to reopen (support reply, 15:35 UTC), so Loupe trains on the Serverless Training API, where our loop streams the examples (no upload), saves a sampler checkpoint and promotes it to an ordinary LoRA model that §12 deploys as before. The serverless trainer bills Glimmer at US$5.86 a million training tokens unless the free offer covers it (unconfirmed, asked of Fireworks), so every run is held to a spend cap. The tuned model is named Loupe (operator request, 2026-10-28 15:59 SAST; the name proposed and taken at 16:00 SAST: a jeweller's loupe tells a real stone from a fake, and it sits beside Glimmer and Spark): each training run is one version numbered major.minor (operator, 2026-10-28 16:03 SAST): Loupe 1.0 first, then 1.1, 1.2 and on for each retrain, written `isitlegit-loupe-1-0` on Fireworks (model IDs take no dots) and "Loupe 1.0 (Muse Glimmer 30B, LoRA)" in documents; the major number moves to 2.0 only for a new base model or a changed `PageScreen` contract, and a version number is never reused, a failed run's included. Renaming before 1.0 trains costs one plan edit; after it, the trained model keeps its name.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Write `scripts/glimmer/train_serverless.py`. Done when: its tests prove the date, file, spend and resume checks.
2. Train, promote and record. Done when: the promoted model's ID and the run's billed amount are recorded.

- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/train_serverless.py` (the SDK in `~/.venvs/fireworks`) that opens a LoRA session on `accounts/fireworks/models/muse-glimmer-30b` (Fireworks' default rank), streams `train.jsonl` in batches as cross-entropy examples built with the session tokenizer (screenshots as `ImageChunk` in `image` mode), steps Adam once a batch for one epoch, saves a training checkpoint every 200 steps for resume, saves the sampler checkpoint `loupe-1-0` (at most 17 characters) at the end, and promotes it to the model `isitlegit-loupe-1-0`. Done when: `tests/scripts/test_train_serverless.py` shows the calls, in order, against a fake SDK. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/training-api/serverless (sessions, checkpoints, promotion). **Corrected 2026-10-28:** was `scripts/glimmer/train.ts` uploading a dataset for a managed job. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation):** the loop itself is the Fireworks cookbook's `recipes/sft_loop.py` in serverless mode (session, batches, Adam, DCP checkpoints every 200 steps, resume from them, the final checkpoint and its promotion), which the script configures and wraps; the test drives the wrapper against a fake loop and the real `Config` was constructed with every field the script passes. The cookbook writes step metrics only to its W&B logger, so the script wraps that logger to record them (marked `WORKAROUND` in the code).
- [x] Refuse to start when the tokens to send, counted with the session tokenizer, times US$5.86 a million would pass the cap of US$60 for a run (the Fireworks budget is US$90 in all, design spec section 11), and stop the loop when the running count reaches it, saving a checkpoint first. Until 2026-11-30 23:59 SAST the cap is lifted, but only while the `Probe` reconciliation below reads US$0 rated for training (proof that Glimmer's supervised fine-tuning is free under the offer); a reconciliation that reads more, or none yet, keeps US$60. From 2026-12-01 the US$60 cap applies again by the date in the code, with no step to remember. Done when: `tests/scripts/test_train_serverless.py` refuses an oversized dataset and stops a run at the cap with a checkpoint saved when the cap applies, runs it whole on 2026-11-30 with a US$0 reconciliation, and applies the cap again on 2026-12-01 and with a nonzero or missing reconciliation. **Corrected 2026-10-28 (operator, 20:50 SAST: "lift the cap until 30 November, just remember to put it back"):** the cap held 1.0 to a few thousand screenshot examples; the lift's end date is in the code, and the decision line under `Decisions` in `docs/glimmer/training.md` names it.

## 12. Deployment with 5-minute scale to zero

Serve the tuned model from one dedicated deployment with live merge, zero minimum replicas, one maximum and a 5-minute scale-to-zero window (the operator's limits; Fireworks' minimum). Every change sends an update mask, because an update without one zeroes every unmentioned field.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

> **Started:** 2026-10-28T21:11:05Z

**Build order:**

1. Write `scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts`. Done when: its tests prove masks and read-back.
2. Deploy and record. Done when: the deployment's settings are read back and recorded.

- [x] Write `ensure` in `scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts` creating, or getting if it exists, a deployment of the tuned model with live merge on one H100 80 GB, `minReplicaCount` 0, `maxReplicaCount` 1 and `scaleToZeroWindow` 5 minutes, reading the admin key through `conclave-op`. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-deploy.test.ts` shows the create body and that a second `ensure` creates nothing. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras, https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation, against https://docs.fireworks.ai/fine-tuning/deploying-loras):** live merge deploys the fine-tuned model itself as `baseModel` with the `default` deployment shape, which Fireworks fills with a shape that can merge it; the GPU it picks is read back and recorded rather than asked for.
- [x] Send every update with an explicit update mask and refuse to build an update request without one. Done when: the test proves the refusal. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/api-reference/training-sdk/deployment-management **Corrected 2026-10-28 (implementation, against https://docs.fireworks.ai/api-reference/update-deployment):** the REST `PATCH` has no update-mask parameter and changes what its body carries, so `updateBody()` requires the mask and sends exactly the fields it names.

## 13. Warm-up and held-out accuracy measured

Measure what the runtime design depends on: how long warm-up really takes, that the deployment really scales to zero after 5 minutes, and whether the tuned Glimmer beats Spark on the held-out set. Every session keeps the day's running time within 30 minutes through a local ledger.

## 14. Daily 30-minute runtime ledger

> **Started:** 2026-10-31T17:30:00Z

> **Plan corrected 2026-10-31 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s14.md`):** the ledger counts every reservation at its full reserve until it is settled, so an interrupted caller, a timed-out request or a lost answer is never undercounted; admission and reservation are one atomic statement, idempotent on the caller's id; each reservation is split across the SAST days it touches; the tail is the measured scale-down, not the 300 s setting; the rate is checked for the deployed GPU; and the local callers that wake the deployment (`deploy.ts`'s key check and warm-up, not `measure.ts`) move onto this ledger, with their earlier wakes imported once.

Fireworks has no daily runtime cap, so the Worker holds the deployment to 30 minutes a day by deciding when to stop sending requests: with no requests, the deployment scales to zero 5 minutes later on its own. The inference-only key cannot scale the deployment, and the admin key never enters the Worker. Phase 3 (the vision phase) so it is ready before the monitor (phase 6) needs it.

**Phase:** 3
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Add the ledger table and `src/vision/glimmer-minutes.ts`. Done when: tests count bursts and refuse at the limit.
2. Share it with the local scripts. Done when: `deploy.ts`'s key check and warm-up reserve and settle on the same ledger.

- [x] Add migration `0056_glimmer_runtime.sql` (number from the coordinator, 2026-10-31): `glimmer_runtime`, one row per reservation per SAST day it touches (`id`, `reservation_id`, `day`, `source` worker, local or import, `caller`, `purpose`, `wake_at`, `reserved_minutes`, `used_minutes` null until settled, `last_answer_at`, `settled_at`). Done when: it applies locally (`pnpm db:migrate:local`). **Done 2026-10-31:** applied locally (`pnpm db:migrate:local`); the remote apply is the coordinator's at the deploy that ships §14.

- [x] Write `minutesByDay(at, minutes)` and `minutesUsed(rows, day)` in `src/vision/glimmer-minutes.ts`: a reservation counts its used minutes once settled and its reserved minutes until then, each SAST day its own part. Done when: the test computes two recorded bursts exactly and charges a burst across SAST midnight to both days; `scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts` imports the same `minutesByDay`. **Done 2026-10-31:** `minutesByDay and minutesUsed` in the test; `deploy.ts` re-exports the same function.
- [x] Write `mayCall(state, request)` (the pure rule) and `reserveBurst(db, reservation)` (the atomic one): refuse when any day part would take its day past 30 minutes, or the total at the rate past US$90; a new burst reserves the measured maximum warm-up, its 60-second margin, the calls it expects and the tail. One `WITH parts ... INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE` statement checks and inserts every day part, so two callers can never both take the last minutes; a retried reservation with the same id is answered from its first rows, never charged twice. Done when: the test refuses at both edges and allows just under them, two concurrent reservations for the last minutes give one success, a repeated id adds nothing, and a total at US$90 refuses with minutes left in the day. **Done 2026-10-31:** `mayCall` and `reserveBurst on D1` in the test (both edges, the second day across midnight, concurrent last minutes, a repeated id, the total at US$90, a pricier GPU's day money). Mutations leaving out the warm-up or the tail, or counting an unsettled reservation as nothing, each fail it.
- [x] Write `settleBurst(db, id, lastActivity)`: used minutes run from the wake to the later of the last answer and the last request's deadline, plus the tail, never more than reserved unless the caller reports more; settling twice changes nothing. Any D1 error is a typed `ledger-unavailable` refusal (fail closed: no request is sent on an unreadable ledger), and an uncertain write is read back by id. Done when: the test charges a timed-out request to its deadline, settles once, and refuses with nothing sent when D1 fails. **Done 2026-10-31:** `settleBurst on D1` in the test (settled once, a timed-out request charged to its deadline, a burst past its reserve across midnight, a failed write keeping the reserve counted); `reserveBurst` refuses with nothing reserved on a failing ledger and reads back a lost answer.

Done when: tests with a stand-in runner show a local wake and a Worker burst on the same day refused past one shared total, and a repeated import adding nothing. **Done 2026-10-31:** `test/node/glimmer-deploy.test.ts` runs the same statements on an in-memory SQLite copy of migration 0056: a Worker burst of 20 minutes refuses a local 12-minute warm-up, two wakes for the last minutes give one reservation, a failing ledger refuses with nothing sent, and a second `importWakes` adds nothing. The stand-in is SQLite, not wrangler: `wranglerExec` is `execute` from `scripts/lib/meta-budget.ts`, already proven live by the teacher runs.

## 15. Warm-up handling and wake call

> **Started:** 2026-10-31T18:10:00Z

> **Plan corrected 2026-10-31 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s15.md`):** the wake reserves the whole retry window before its first request; one deadline per burst, kept in the ledger row, is shared by the wake and every screening call and survives a Workflow re-entry; every request has its own deadline; readiness needs a valid completion; the outcomes are typed; the notice is deduplicated in D1; and the recovery command names the version.

A scaled-to-zero deployment answers the first request with an immediate `503 DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP` and queues nothing. This section provides `wakeGlimmer`, which the monitor run calls at its start so warm-up overlaps the DNS checks (that call is D05 T04 §2's, the file that owns the run), and the retry the screener uses until the measured warm-up limit, after which Spark takes the page.

**Phase:** 3
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Write `wakeGlimmer` and the retry. Done when: tests cover a warm-up that ends in time and one that does not.
2. Handle a missing deployment. Done when: a 404 gives its reason and one operator notice.

- [x] Write `wakeGlimmer(ctx)` in `src/vision/glimmer-wake.ts`: reserve the burst first (`reserveBurst`, §14) for the measured maximum warm-up, its 60-second margin, the calls expected and the tail, with an id from the monitor run, so a re-entered Workflow step finds the same burst and its deadline (the row's `wake_at` plus warm-up plus margin) instead of starting a new wait; then send one 1-token request with a 10-second deadline and return `{ burst, deadline }` without waiting for the warm-up, so the DNS work runs meanwhile. Done when: `test/vision/glimmer-wake.test.ts` shows no request when the day's minutes are spent, the wake returning before the stubbed warm-up ends, and a re-entry reusing the first deadline. **Done 2026-10-31:** `test/vision/glimmer-wake.test.ts` (`wakeGlimmer`): no request with the day's minutes spent, the whole retry window reserved, one request and a return before the warm-up ends, a re-entry finding the first deadline with no request.
- [x] Write `untilWarm(ctx, burst)`: retry `503` with `DEPLOYMENT_SCALING_UP`, `429`, other `5xx` and network errors with exponential backoff (5 seconds doubling, capped at 30), each attempt cut at its own deadline and never past the burst's, until a valid completion (status 200 with `choices[0].message`), or the burst's deadline, then `warming` for Spark to take the page; after the deadline it answers `warming` at once with no request. Typed outcomes: `ready`, `warming`, `refused` (the ledger), `deployment_missing`, `unauthorized` (401 or 403, not retried), `bad_answer` (a 200 that is not a completion), `not_configured` (no key or gateway URL, no request); the burst is settled on every path. Done when: the test's stubbed warm-up ends in time once and runs over once, asserts the backoff schedule and the deadline, and shows an empty 200 is not ready, a 401 is final and a hanging request is cut. Source: https://docs.fireworks.ai/deployments/autoscaling **Done 2026-10-31:** `untilWarm` in the test: ready after 503s, a 429 and a 502 with sleeps 5, 10, 20, 30, 30, 30 s; warming at the deadline and at once after it with no request; an empty 200 a bad answer; 401 and 403 final; a hanging request cut at the burst's deadline. Treating a 503 as final fails 6 of its 11 tests.
- [x] Return `deployment_missing` on a 404 (Fireworks deletes deployments idle for 7 days) and send the operator one notice through `sendMail` (`src/mail/smtp2go.ts`) with the send key `glimmer-deployment-missing:<deployment>:<SAST day>`, kept in D1 like the Meta cap notices, so concurrent calls and a fresh Worker send it once and a failed send is claimed again by the next miss; it names `node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts ensure <version>` with the deployed version from `glimmer-caps.ts`. Done when: the test's 404 gives the reason and one notice across two calls, a failed send leaves it to the next, and the named command parses with `deploy.ts`'s `parseCommand`. **Done 2026-10-31:** `a missing deployment` in the test (one notice across two calls, naming `node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts ensure 1.0`; a failed send left to the next miss); `test/node/glimmer-deploy.test.ts` parses `recoveryCommand()` with `parseCommand`.
- [x] Take the transport from the Worker's environment: the `custom-fireworks` AI Gateway route (`GLIMMER_GATEWAY_URL`) and `FIREWORKS_API_KEY`, both set by the coordinator at the deploy that ships §1 (asked 2026-10-31 19:18 SAST); every request carries the gateway's metadata-only log headers, as `metaResponses` does. Done when: the test sees the headers on every request and `not_configured` with no request when either is missing; the live one-token call through the route is §1's first item. **Done 2026-10-31:** the test checks the gateway URL, `cf-aig-collect-log-payload: false`, both keys and the model on every request, and `not_configured` naming what is missing with no request. Not yet bound in `wrangler.jsonc` or `Env`: `GLIMMER_GATEWAY_URL` and `FIREWORKS_API_KEY` are set with §1's deploy, which also proves the route live.
- [x] Commit: `vision: Glimmer warm-up handling and wake call`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/vision/glimmer-wake.test.ts` passes and fails if a `503` during warm-up is treated as a final failure instead of retried.
