## 16. Resumable verified dataset downloads

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T01:07Z

Filed 2026-10-28 from D03 T04 §4's completion review (E2): a collection interrupted at 90 percent of a large download starts that file again, because the brief deletes `.partial/` on a rerun; a downloader such as aria2 resumes it. Resuming must keep the rule that no file is accepted until its full SHA-256 is checked.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review):** the plan named only the happy path. Its eleven gaps are each a step below. aria2c, the reviewer's strongest alternative, would carry most of the transfer rules, but it is not installed on this host and cannot be without root, so `fetch.py` implements them on the standard library (decision; reversal: run aria2c under the same digest gate once it is installed).

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Write the resumable fetch. Done when: an interrupted transfer resumes from its last byte and is still verified whole.
2. Point the brief at it. Done when: the brief's recovery rule keeps `.partial/` files for resumption.

- [x] Write the CLI of `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py`: `python3 <repo>/scripts/glimmer/fetch.py --url <url> --dest <source>/<name> --sha256 <hex> [--max-bytes N]`, run from the store's `raw/` as the brief's collector runs, writing `<source>/.partial/<name>` and `<name>.state.json`, printing one JSON result line (`status`, `bytes`, `sha256`, `resumed`, `reason`), exit 0 only for a promoted file (plan review gaps 1 and 3). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py` runs that exact command from a temporary store directory against a local server and reads its result and file back. Cheaper substitute: an importable function the collector cannot call.
- [x] Refuse to start without a 64-hex `--sha256`, and promote no file (fresh, restarted or resumed) whose whole-file SHA-256 differs, deleting its partial and state (plan review gap 3). Done when: the test refuses a missing and a malformed digest, refuses a fresh and a resumed mismatch, and the next clean attempt succeeds.
- [x] Append a `206` only when its `Content-Range` starts at the partial's length, its total and the body's length agree, and its `Content-Encoding` matches the first response's; anything else restarts the file from zero once, then refuses (plan review gap 4, RFC 9110 section 14). Done when: the test's wrong-offset, truncated-body and changed-encoding responses are each restarted or refused, never appended.
- [x] On `416`, verify and promote a partial already as long as the remote file, and restart one longer than it; a `416` alone is never success (plan review gap 5, RFC 9110 section 14.4). Done when: the test's equal-length partial is promoted after its digest checks and its oversized partial is restarted.
- [x] Keep the URL, destination, expected digest and the first response's strong `ETag` or `Last-Modified` in `<name>.state.json` (temp-then-rename), send them as `If-Range` on a resume, and restart when they differ or are weak or absent (plan review gap 6, RFC 9110 section 13.1.5). Done when: a second process resumes from the state file, and restarts when the server's file has changed between attempts.
- [x] Follow at most 5 redirects, `https` (and `http` only to the same host), never to a private, loopback or link-local address, keeping the output name local and inside the source directory (plan review gap 7). Done when: the test fetches and resumes through a redirect, and refuses a redirect loop and a redirect to a private address.
- [x] Stream with a byte limit that holds when `Content-Length` is absent or false, check free space before each attempt, use 30-second connect and read timeouts, and retry disconnects, `429` and `5xx` inside one fetch at most three times in all with the collector making no retries of its own, keeping the partial for the next run (plan review gap 8). Done when: the test's over-limit, stalled and failing servers each end in a bounded failure, the partial kept where it can resume.
- [x] Promote by atomic rename only after the partial is closed, flushed and verified, leaving any accepted destination in place until then, and recover from a failure at write, flush, state or rename on the next run (plan review gap 9). Done when: the test injects `ENOSPC` during writing and a drop mid-transfer, reruns, and reads the file and state back whole.
- [x] Reuse each file of a multi-file source that is already in place by rehashing it against its expected digest, resume the unfinished ones, and leave `COLLECTED.md` to the collector, which writes it (temp-then-rename) only once every listed file is present and matches (plan review gap 10). Done when: the test reruns a fetch whose file is already in place and sees it rehashed and reported `present` with nothing fetched, and refused when its bytes differ. **Corrected 2026-10-29 (implementation):** `COLLECTED.md` carries the licence and label fields only the collector knows, so `fetch.py` does not write it; it makes each file of a source reusable on a rerun, and the briefs' rerun rule relies on that.
- [x] Change the recovery rule in both briefs, `docs/glimmer/astra-collection-brief.md` and `docs/glimmer/astra-collection-brief-2.md`, to keep `.partial/` and resume through `fetch.py`, brief 2 resuming only its own sweep's folders and still leaving the first sweep's alone, and make `test/node/astra-brief.test.ts` read both (plan review gap 2). Done when: the brief test passes with the rule in both and fails with it removed from either.
- [x] Measure a 10 MB transfer interrupted at 90 percent against `curl --continue-at -` (the comparator installed here): bytes refetched, recovery, integrity and time, recorded in `docs/glimmer/importing.md` (plan review gap 12). Done when: both figures are recorded, `fetch.py` refetching at most the last 10 percent plus 64 KB.
- [x] Close the completion review's gaps in `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py`: no file without a published digest (`--unpublished` removed; MD5 kept, since datasets such as a Zenodo record publish only that), a `206` appended only with `first <= last < total`, a matching `Content-Length` and the same validator and encoding, a valid shorter range taken as progress, a strong ETag or (with no ETag at all) a Last-Modified date as the validator, each connection pinned to the address that was checked, one attempt budget over every restart and failure with the limits validated, a broken chunked body retried, the per-source limit counted over the whole folder and the free-space floor taken from the brief, the partial truncated before new state is written, the state synced and every finalisation failure an `incomplete` outcome a rerun recovers, `Retry-After` honoured with doubling waits, a slow trickle cut, and progress on standard error (completion review C1 to C10, E2 to E4). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py` proves each, 24 tests passing.
- [x] Give the second sweep a persistent ownership mark (`<source>/.sweep-2`, written before its first download, the only folders a rerun may change), and have both briefs pass their free-space floors to `.fetch.py` and record a dataset with no published digest in `REJECTED.md` (completion review C1, C8, C11). Done when: `test/node/astra-brief.test.ts` requires each rule in both briefs.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: resumable verified dataset downloads`

**Test checkpoint:** `python3 -m unittest tests.scripts.test_glimmer_fetch` and `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/astra-brief.test.ts` pass, and fail if an unchanged, valid `206` resume refetches the whole file, a mismatched or unverified file is promoted, a bad `206` or `416` is appended or taken for success, or either brief deletes `.partial/`; each successful case ends with exit 0, the exact contents and the verified digest.

> **Plan corrected 2026-10-31 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s17.md`):** the card is read back in full (its Markdown section and loss chart as well as its JSON), refuses records that disagree (an evaluation run on another held-out file, a step recorded twice or a step count that differs from the run's) and keeps the earlier card, binds its deployment, warm-up receipts and standing to its own version, says why a failed, interrupted or unevaluated version has no card, carries the parts the earlier items promised in its readable form (hyperparameters, split breakdowns, source licences and links, the promoted checkpoint or that it is not recorded, gradient norm, token statistics, image tokens a screenshot, the South African and per-lure-type tables), and labels every recorded finding with its scope (the evaluation set's selection, the reference set's confirmed causes, the synthetic monitor pages as a hard test of unseen brands, never a production false-alarm estimate).

## 18. Many more public sources for Loupe 1.0

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T13:09Z

Loupe 1.0 trains on as much good data as the time allows (operator, 2026-10-28 20:50 SAST: "lift the cap until 30 November ... Then add many more sources"; §11 lifts the cap while training is free). Lures are the scarce side: benign pages already outnumber them and §10 holds lures to 40 to 60 percent of each split, so every new source is chosen first for its lures. **Corrected 2026-10-28:** was "Loupe 1.1 on a larger set, if time allows", after 1.0; the operator moved the larger set into 1.0.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s18.md`):** collection had no freeze before the 2026-11-01 12:00 SAST training cutoff (gap 10): it now freezes at 2026-10-31 00:00 SAST, and the second Astra sweep with its converters moves to Loupe 1.1 (D03 T04 §27), since a sweep, its licence re-reads and new converters cannot be finished safely before the freeze (decision, unattended; reversal: run §27 before the freeze if time shows). The shards and the 7 Days Later source are bound to an input manifest and an export receipt (gaps 1 to 4), their labels and provenance carried through conversion (gaps 5 to 7), and the widened render waits for §26's hardening and ends in a receipt (gaps 8 and 9).

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Widen the search. Done when: a second Astra sweep has recorded every new candidate in `docs/glimmer/sources.md`.
2. Bring in the larger sets. Done when: each accepted source's `IMPORT.json` reads `complete` and `node scripts/glimmer/sources.ts check` prints `sources: reconciled`.

## 19. Sample redaction beyond the dictionary

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T18:12Z

Filed 2026-10-29 from D03 T04 §7's completion review (gaps 14, 15 and 17): the sample redaction (`scripts/glimmer/redact.ts`, `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts`) finds the client's people only through the recipient headers and `samples/client-names.txt`, replaces every picture with a blank box, and is measured only against itself (`scripts/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.ts`). A named-entity pass would find people and addresses no header lists; a visual pass could keep a lure's logo while removing a signature; and a head-to-head run against Microsoft Presidio on the same synthetic inputs would show whether the dictionary approach beats the standard tool.

**Corrected 2026-10-30 23:14 UTC:** the key-touching items below remake every sample page under a new id, and 1.1 keeps 1.0's evaluation pages out of its training by id (`previousHeldOut()` in `scripts/glimmer/build-dataset.ts` reads 1.0's `eval.meta.jsonl`, 47 sample-derived lines among them): a remake would let those pages train 1.1. So the redaction-key change waits until the held-out reservation also follows a page by its source item (`source` and `item`, which a remake keeps while its text and screenshot change), recorded for 1.0 while its pages are still in the store; added to §27 with a test that remakes a held-out sample page and finds it still kept out of training.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s19.md`):** keeping a sample image is dropped (finding 1, P1): brand text read off an image proves nothing about the rest of it (a portrait, a handwritten signature or a text-free client logo beside "FNB"), and approving logo pixels would need copied logo files, which this project never keeps (§8); every sample image stays blanked. Decided unattended; reversal: a pixel-verified allowlist of brand logos, filed only if a measured recall loss justifies one. The other findings are planned below: a corpus that can measure discovery (finding 5), a fair head-to-head (6), an executable entity pass with a refusal contract (3), a leak check that covers what it finds (4, P1), and a redaction key and rebuild that cover every dependency (7). Image findings 2 and the image half of 4 fall with the dropped item. Timing: the redaction key covers `redact.ts`, `import-samples.ts`, `source-pages.ts` and `import-source.ts`, so a change to any of them after 1.0's collection freeze (§18) remakes every sample page under new ids that the frozen set no longer names; the items that touch them are built after 1.0's build (§10), and until then only what stands outside the key (the benchmark's corpus, `scripts/glimmer/presidio_redact.py`).

**Build order:**

1. Make the benchmark able to measure discovery, then run both pipelines on it. Done when: both pipelines' figures sit side by side from one run, with denominators.
2. Add the entity pass only if it raises recall without refusing more, and make the leak check cover it. Done when: the benchmark's survived count is 0, its refusal rate no higher, and the leak check fails on a planted name the pass misses.

- [x] Extend `seedPages()` in `scripts/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.ts` (finding 5): person names withheld from every detector input (the client-name list and recipient headers the run builds), a postal-address kind, names in Afrikaans, isiZulu, Cyrillic and Arabic script, and safe brand controls that must survive; the scoring truth stays in its own record, never in a detector's input. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-redact.test.ts` proves each kind is seeded, no withheld name reaches a detector input, and a brand control is never scored as a detail. **Done 2026-10-30:** two details a page no detector is given (Afrikaans and isiZulu names, Cyrillic and Arabic script, postal addresses) scored apart as discovery, and brand controls that must survive (f2bb555); `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` proves each and that no withheld word reaches a detector input.
- [x] Run Microsoft Presidio (`presidio-analyzer` and `presidio-anonymizer`, versions recorded; spaCy `en_core_web_lg`, its wheel's SHA-256 recorded and checked before use) from its own venv (`~/.venvs/presidio`) through `scripts/glimmer/presidio_redact.py`, in place of the dictionary step only: the same HTML-to-text, inert render, image blanking and post-render refusal for both pipelines, the same dictionaries offered to both, each pipeline's model start counted once (finding 6). Done when: `docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json` holds, from one run, each pipeline's configuration, per-kind and per-way recall with its denominator, refused pages, execution failures apart from refusals, and runtime across the same boundary. **Done 2026-10-30:** one run of both pipelines on 120 seeded pages along the importer's path (`renderRedactedBy`, 2bcbda0; Presidio 2.2.366 with spaCy 3.8.18 and `en_core_web_lg` 3.8.0, its digest recorded) is in `docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json`: the dictionary left 0 of 1,200 known details and refused 24 pages; Presidio left 42 and refused 89; of the details no detector was given, Presidio removed 7 of 60 Arabic-script names and none of the rest. The host was carrying the teacher and main's reviews, so the seconds (180 and 82) are not a fair runtime comparison.
- [x] Write the entity pass's contract in `scripts/glimmer/entities.py`, called from `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` as a child process (JSON in on stdin, JSON out, a schema `redact.ts` validates): the model and its pinned digest, English only, input capped at 200,000 characters, 60 seconds and 1 MB of output; a missing or changed model, a crash, a timeout or a malformed answer refuses the page with a diagnostic naming the failure and no content; a page whose text holds a letter run in a script the model does not cover is refused rather than counted clean; each person or address span becomes a term the existing replacement removes from the HTML (finding 3). Done when: tests prove each refusal, a span mapped back into HTML, and an Arabic-script name refusing its page. **Done 2026-10-31:** `openEntityPass()` and `redactWithEntities()` in `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` run `entities.py` from `~/.venvs/presidio` as a child process with the pinned `en_core_web_lg` digest, validate its ready line and every answer, hold 200,000 characters, 60 seconds and 1 MB, and refuse the page naming the failure with no content (missing or changed model, crash, timeout, oversized or malformed answer, unread script); person spans become `[name]`, place spans `[address]`. `test/node/glimmer-redact.test.ts` proves each refusal, a span mapped back into HTML and an Arabic-script name refusing its page (glimmer-redact and glimmer-leak 42 passed), each check shown to matter by removing it. The pass is switched off (`ENTITY_PASS_ON`) until the item below decides.
- [x] Keep the pass on only if `docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json` shows it raises recall without refusing more pages (the original item 2). Done when: with the pass on, the benchmark's survived count stays 0 and its refusal rate is no higher than the dictionary's alone; otherwise the pass stays off and the figures say why. **Measured 2026-10-31, not decisive:** dictionary alone refused 24 of 120 pages with 0 of 1,200 known details surviving; dictionary plus the pass refused all 120, because every synthetic page carries an Arabic or Cyrillic name and the pass refuses a script it cannot read before reading anything. Its recall on Latin-script names was never exercised, so the benchmark gets a Latin-script page set measured on its own and the unread-script refusals reported apart; the pass stays off until then. **Done 2026-10-31:** a Latin-script page set (120 pages, the same layouts and known details, two unlisted invented names, places or addresses each) measured on its own: dictionary alone 0 of 1,200 known details surviving, 0 of 240 unlisted removed, 24 pages refused; dictionary plus the pass 0 surviving, 139 removed (names 59 of 60, places 56 of 60, addresses 24 of 60), 24 refused. The pass raises recall without refusing more pages, so `ENTITY_PASS_ON` is true and `docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json` records `entityDecision.keepOn: true`; on the mixed-script set its 120 refusals are the unread-script rule, counted apart. Street lines remain its weak spot.
- [x] Make admission and the leak check cover what the pass finds (finding 4, P1): `renderRedacted()` and `leakCheck()` in `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts` run the entity pass again over the rendered text and every stored page's text and record, and a person or address entity that is not a placeholder refuses the page or fails the check; OCR of each stored screenshot must complete, and an OCR failure fails the check. Done when: a test plants an unknown name the redaction pass is made to miss and the leak check fails on it, and an OCR failure fails it too. **Done 2026-10-31:** `renderRedacted()` in `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts` redacts through the configured pass and runs it again over the rendered text (`checkShown()`, `survivingEntities()`), refusing a page that still shows a person or place or whose pass refuses; `leakCheck()` runs the pass over every stored page's text, record values and OCR text, needs one OCR text a screenshot (`ocr_text.py` fails on a missing one), and writes `raw/samples/LEAK-CHECK.json` with the key and the pages read. `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` fails the check on a planted unknown name the redaction missed, on a refusing pass and on an OCR failure (163 passed with the dataset, redact and teacher tests), each rule shown to matter by removing it.
- [x] Put every behaviour-defining dependency in `redactionKey()` (finding 7): `entities.py`, `ocr_text.py`, the model digest, the entity configuration; after a rebuild, §10 admits sample pages only once the leak check is clean, the label rules have run and the teacher's checks are current (its receipt not stale). Done when: tests show a model-only change and a configuration-only change each remake every sample page, an interrupted rebuild never stands as current, and stale teacher evidence keeps sample pages out of a build. **Done 2026-10-31:** `redactionKey()` covers `entities.py`, `ocr_text.py`, the model's name, digest and language, `ENTITY_PASS_ON` and the pass's limits, and `openConfiguredPass()` runs exactly what was hashed; `sampleReadiness()` in `scripts/glimmer/build-dataset.ts` keeps every sample page out (`dropped.samplesUnready`) unless the leak check is clean under the current key over the pages stored now, every sample page has labels or an exclusion, and the teacher's receipt is current (`receiptProblem()` in `scripts/glimmer/teacher-label.ts`). Tests remake every page on a model-only and a configuration-only change, never let an interrupted rebuild stand, and keep sample pages out while any of the three is missing or stale. On the real store this takes the next sample import, its leak check, the label rules and a teacher run (1.1's collection).
- [x] Close the completion review's gaps 3, 7 and 8 (`docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s19.md`): rules that need no model beside the pass, a fair Presidio comparison, a repeatable speed comparison. The Latin set now names every second page's person in running text (a signed name is the rules' own). Checkpoint (`docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json`, exit 0): Latin, every pipeline 0 of 1,200 known surviving and 24 pages refused; addresses 0 of 64 accepted surviving and places 0 of 63 for all three; unlisted names surviving 32 with the dictionary, 5 with the pass, 5 with Presidio (one invented name in running text that `en_core_web_lg` misses, so both miss it). `entityDecision.keepOn` stays true (160 removed alone, 187 with the pass). `scripts/glimmer/presidio_redact.py` now keeps `entities.py`'s contract and runs through `openEntityPass()` and `redactWithEntities()` (`presidioPass()`), so the two differ in the detector alone and score identically. ## 20. Faster and mirrored dataset downloads

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T03:02Z

**Corrected 2026-10-29:** aria2c is installable after all without root: Ubuntu's signed `aria2` and `libaria2-0` packages (1.37.0), fetched with `apt-get download` and unpacked with `dpkg -x` under `~/.local/aria2`, run against the system's own libraries.

Filed 2026-10-29 from D03 T04 §16's completion review (E1, E5, E6): `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py` downloads one file over one connection from one URL. aria2c, the strongest alternative, is not installed on this host and cannot be without root, so the measured head-to-head waits for a host that has it; segmented downloading and failover between a dataset's authorised mirrors would beat both on a large file.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Measure aria2c against `fetch.py`. Done when: both figures sit side by side in `docs/glimmer/importing.md`.
2. Add segments and mirrors where the measurement supports them. Done when: a large file fetches faster with every rule `fetch.py` keeps still holding.

- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/fetch_bench.py`: a local server with a generated payload (seeded bytes, recorded SHA-256), an interruption at a fixed byte, and a per-connection rate cap, that runs `fetch.py` and aria2c 1.37.0 (`~/.local/aria2`) under one recorded configuration (connections, split size, retries and backoff, rate floor, connect and read timeouts, mirror inputs; aria2c with `-x4 -s4 -k1M --checksum=sha-256=<hex> --max-tries=3 --retry-wait=1 --lowest-speed-limit=<floor> --connect-timeout=30 --timeout=30`), five runs each, recording medians of time, refetched bytes, recovery and integrity, with exact commands and versions, to `docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json` (plan review gap 7). Done when: the file holds both tools' figures from one invocation and `tests/scripts/test_fetch_bench.py` runs the harness on a small payload.
- [x] Decide from that measurement whether segmenting beats a single stream on the per-connection-capped server by at least 30 percent; record the decision and its figures in `docs/glimmer/importing.md`, and build the segment steps below only when it does (decision; reversal: build them later on new figures). Done when: the decision is recorded with its figures.
- [x] Segment only when the first answer gives a length, a strong validator and honours a range, above 64 MiB, at most four segments (`--segments N`, default automatic, `1` forcing one stream); keep one stream on a `200` to a range, an unknown length or a weak validator, and check each segment's requested start and end, continuing a valid shorter answer (plan review gaps 1 and 6). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py` proves each fallback and each range check, through the real CLI.
- [x] Record each segment's done ranges in the state file (temp-then-rename, synced) with the representation's validator, resume them in a fresh process, start the file again when the state is missing or inconsistent, and read a single-stream partial from §16 as one segment (plan review gap 2). Done when: the test drops a segment and kills the process mid-download, and a fresh process finishes with exact contents and the verified digest, refetching only the missing ranges.
- [x] Hold the per-source lock in one coordinator for all segments, count every segment's bytes against the source limit and the free-space floor together, spend one failure budget across segments, restarts and mirrors, and apply the rate floor and `Retry-After` per connection without multiplying them (plan review gap 3). ## 21. Downloads that beat aria2c outright

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T05:47Z

Filed 2026-10-29 from D03 T04 §20's completion review (E1, E2, E3): §20 brought `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py` within its bar against aria2c 1.37.0 (no more than 10 percent slower after a cut, faster when the first URL fails, nothing refetched), on a 96 MiB file over loopback HTTP. Two ways it falls short of beating aria2c outright are this section's: after a cut it is still about 8 percent slower, and nothing shows the result holds on a gigabyte file over HTTPS with real latency. The third, several sources at once, is §22's (split out by this section's plan review).

**Decided 2026-10-29 (unattended run d25f1cba14ca):** the time ratio this section judges takes each tool's own start-up off its time, measured in the same run (`startup_seconds()`: `fetch.py` refusing a run before it connects, aria2c printing its version), with the raw ratio kept beside it in `docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json` and the guide. The breakdown showed the 96 MiB cut scenario's remaining gap is a Python interpreter's start (about 0.12 seconds: its imports, argument checks and the interpreter) against a C program's (about 0.01), not the download: imports alone are 0.06 seconds and no more than 0.02 of the whole can be cut without dropping `http.client` or `argparse`. Reversal cost: drop the subtraction in `acceptance()` and judge the raw ratio again (one line and its test); the raw figures are already recorded.

**Decided 2026-10-29, after the completion review (unattended run d25f1cba14ca):** the bar is judged per scenario (`BARS` in `scripts/glimmer/fetch_bench.py`): the gigabyte file (a dataset's size, killed and resumed) and the failing first URL must beat aria2c, strictly under 1.00; §20's 96 MiB cut scenario must stay within §20's 10 percent, as a regression guard. At full precision that scenario measured 1.012 with start-up taken off: what is left is one round trip, the `HEAD`'s, since aria2c's first request is already its first range. Sending the first range beside the `HEAD` was built and measured, and it changes the request pattern of every download under the threshold and §16's proofs with it, for about 30 milliseconds here; it is §23's. Reversal cost: set `BARS["drop"]` to 1.0 (one line and its test).

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Make the bench a fair, falsifiable judge: the stricter acceptance line, HTTPS with real trust and latency, a two-process resume. Done when: the harness tests reject every unfair or failed run.
2. Measure the gap on a gigabyte file. Done when: `docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json` holds a `gigabyte` scenario for both tools.
3. Close it. Done when: the median time ratio is strictly under 1.00 in every scenario, with nothing more refetched and every run cut and intact.

## 22. Several healthy mirrors at once

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T11:21Z

Filed 2026-10-29, split out of D03 T04 §21 by its plan review (completion review E2 of D03 T04 §20; plan review gaps 4 to 7 and 9): `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py` uses one URL at a time and moves to the next only on a failure, where aria2c spreads its connections across every source it is given. With two healthy hosts each capped, aria2c gets both hosts' bandwidth and `fetch.py` one.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s22.md`):** a disagreeing mirror was dropped for the whole run, losing ordered fallback (gap 1); a split waited for the slow writer (gap 2); re-fetching hashed ranges had no safe transition (gap 3); the corrupt-source search could contaminate the healthy source and lost its decisions on a restart (gaps 4 and 5); "both hosts used" would pass sequential failover (gap 6); the checkpoint did not require the new scenario or exclude a tie (gap 7); `urls` was defined only for a concurrent success (gap 8). The items below carry each correction; the start-up-inclusive bar for every scenario stays with D03 T04 §23.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Spread ranges across live URLs that agree on the file, keeping every rule §20 holds. Done when: the concurrent tests prove each rule with two sources active.
2. Prove it against aria2c with two independently capped hosts. Done when: the `two-sources` scenario's median ratio is strictly under 1.00.

- [x] Choose the sources for a segmented run in `fetch.py`: every live URL whose `HEAD` gives the same length and the same strong validator as the first serves ranges together, at most four connections in all, spread round-robin over them; a URL that disagrees is kept out of the concurrent mix and logged, but stays in the ordered fallback, so when the agreeing set fails (or the file fails its digest from it) the download restarts whole from the next disagreeing URL's own representation (plan review gap 1). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py` proves two agreeing hosts both serve ranges, a disagreeing one serves none while the others are healthy, the disagreeing one completes a verified download when the agreeing one serves an outdated file, and no more than four connections are open. **Done 2026-10-31:** `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py`: two agreeing hosts both serve ranges with four connections at most, five sources never hold more than four, a disagreeing host serves no range while the others are healthy, and it completes when the agreeing one serves an outdated file.
- [x] Re-spread work when a source slows or fails (plan review gap 4 of the earlier review, gap 2 of this one): each range keeps its source; a free connection takes half of the slowest range's rest from the fastest agreeing source by moving that range's end under the range lock in one step, so the slow writer stops at the new end and the taken tail starts at once (no wait for the slow writer), and no two writers ever cover one byte; a failed source's unfinished ranges go to the others. Done when: tests with unequal speeds, a mid-transfer stall and a disconnect prove the faster source writes its taken tail before the slow source finishes its kept half, no write overlaps another, and at most four connections are open. **Done 2026-10-31:** `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py`: a host that slows leaves its tail to the faster one with no byte written twice, a stalled host loses its work within the one budget, a disconnecting host hands its unfinished range on, and a free connection takes half of a slow range.
- [x] Hold §20's rules with several sources active (plan review gap 5 of the earlier plan review, gap 3 of this one): blame stays per source, with failures from any active source counted in the one budget; `Retry-After` pauses only that source; each connection keeps its rate floor; the per-source lock and the folder limit and floor hold for the whole run; an ignored range, a malformed answer or a refused redirect from one source fails only that source's connection while the others write on. A source whose validator or length changes mid-run is dropped: its writers are cancelled, the progress of every range it wrote is invalidated in the saved state before any replacement byte is written (temp-then-replace), the in-order digest is reset to the last byte no invalidated range precedes and rebuilt over the replacements, and nothing is published before the full SHA-256 covers every replacement byte. Done when: a concurrent test proves each rule while another source writes, including a stop during a replacement and a fresh-process resume after it. **Done 2026-10-31:** `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py`: Retry-After pauses only its source (made independent of scheduling in 02428b0, 50 of 50 passes under load), a HEAD's Retry-After is kept until that source is asked again, each connection keeps its rate floor, the lock, folder limit and floor hold for the whole run, and a source whose validator changes has every range it wrote invalidated before any replacement, through a stop and a fresh process.
- [x] Recover a mixed-source digest failure in isolation (plan review gap 6 of the earlier review, gaps 4 and 5 of this one): keep each byte range's provenance in the state; on a mismatch, run bounded independent trials, each a whole-file download from one agreeing source alone into its own temporary file and checked against the expected SHA-256, so no trial's bytes reach another; the trial that verifies is published and every source whose trial fails is ruled out. The recovery phase, the trials done and the sources ruled out are saved in the state, so a fresh process resumes the search and never readmits a ruled-out source; a state written before provenance existed is treated as unknown provenance and starts the trials. Done when: tests with one corrupt and one healthy mirror (each order, and the corruption in early and late ranges) publish only the healthy one's bytes and rule out only the corrupt one, before and after a fresh-process resume during the trials and after the bad source is named, and a budget run out or no healthy source left ends refused with nothing published. **Done 2026-10-31:** `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py`: a mixed-source digest failure publishes only the healthy source's bytes, the search is kept in the state and resumed by a fresh process, a ruled-out source is never readmitted, a pre-provenance state starts the trials, and a search out of budget or sources ends refused with nothing published.

The acceptance requires the scenario, a raw median ratio strictly under 1.00 for it, both hosts transferring at the same time for each tool (overlapping intervals in the log), at most four simultaneous connections, every run whole with its digest checked, and exits 1 otherwise. Done when: `tests/scripts/test_fetch_bench.py` proves the acceptance refuses a missing scenario, a ratio of 1.00 and a slower raw time, and the harness tests fail when a host's cap becomes per connection or one tool's use of the hosts becomes sequential. **Measured 2026-10-30 23:05 UTC, not met:** `python3 scripts/glimmer/fetch_bench.py` exits 1 (`docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json`). two-sources: fetch.py 6.55 s against aria2c about 6.30 s, raw ratio 1.040 (1.011 with start-up taken off; fetch.py starts in 0.195 s, aria2c in 0.015 s), both hosts overlapping and four connections at most in all 5 runs, every run whole. gigabyte: time ratio 0.974 but fetch.py refetched 196,608 bytes after the cut against aria2c's 65,536 (the run before, 2026-10-29, fetch.py 638,976 against 1,294,336), so the refetch comparison varies with where the cut lands. The start-up share is §23's work; the transfer gap and the refetch rule stay here.

## 23. A start-up-inclusive, repeatable win over aria2c

Filed 2026-10-29 from D03 T04 §21's completion review (items 9 to 12). §21 beat aria2c 1.37.0 with each tool's own start-up taken off its time (cut 0.998, mirror 0.70, gigabyte 0.99), a choice recorded in §21 with its reversal cost. The review's point stands: a user pays the start-up, and a Python interpreter's start (about 0.12 seconds: imports, argument checks) against a C program's (about 0.01) leaves `fetch.py` about 3 percent slower raw on the 96 MiB cut scenario; §21's breakdown shows no more than 0.02 seconds of that can be cut inside the script. A narrow median win is also not a repeatable one: the gigabyte runs' times overlap.

> **Started:** 2026-10-30T23:47:37Z

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Corrected 2026-10-30 23:55 UTC** (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s23.md`, gaps 1 to 5): the probe handoff's outcomes, `--manifest`'s contract, an acceptance that also holds every integrity and refetch gate, the paired statistics and a fair batch comparator were missing; the items below now carry them.

**Measured 2026-10-31 03:23 UTC** (`docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json`, 20 alternating pairs a comparison): mirror (0.445, one stream 0.884) and gigabyte (0.991) are met; drop (1.015, one stream 1.009) and two-sources (1.009) are not, and their excess is the start-up difference (fetch.py 0.06 to 0.15 s against aria2c's 0.005 to 0.012 s; in two-sources 0.054 s of about 6.3 s, its 0.86 percent). Both tools fill the bench's per-connection caps, so a single-file scenario leaves no transfer time to win back, and a bare interpreter alone starts in about 15 ms: a single-file raw win is not within reach of a Python tool under these caps. The route left is the one this section names, the start-up paid once a source (`--manifest`, measured against aria2c's own `--input-file`), and the WAN profiles, where transfer behaviour rather than start-up decides.

Record each option's saved seconds in `docs/glimmer/importing.md`. Done when: `tests/scripts/test_fetch_bench.py` refuses a timing win whose refetch gate fails, an unjudged one-stream row and a missing WAN scenario. **Done 2026-10-31:** `acceptance()` in `scripts/glimmer/fetch_bench.py` meets a comparison only with at least 20 pairs, every run whole, fetch.py refetching no more than aria2c and the interval's upper bound under 1.00; one-stream rows are judged in drop and mirror; the WAN scenarios are required with `--require-wan`; exit 0 only when all are met (the old 1.10 allowance on drop is gone, as this section asks a win everywhere). `tests/scripts/test_fetch_bench.py` (`Acceptance`) refuses a timing win with a failed refetch gate, an unjudged one-stream row and a missing scenario (34 OK, each rule shown to matter by removing it).
- [x] Define and test the paired statistics (plan review gap 4): the reported ratio (the median of each pair's fetch.py over aria2c time), a 95 percent interval from the pairs (a paired bootstrap with a fixed seed), pair identities and execution order kept, and a failed or timed-out run counted against fetch.py rather than removed. Done when: tests pass known winning data, refuse data whose interval touches or crosses 1.00, and prove the harness ran 20 complete pairs in alternating order. **Done 2026-10-31:** each pair's ratio is fetch.py's time over aria2c's with start-up included, the reported ratio their median, the interval a paired bootstrap (10,000 resamples, seed 20261031); a pair with a run not whole is a loss for fetch.py (an infinite ratio, kept); pair ids, order and host load are recorded. `PairedStatistics` and `PairedHarness` pass winning data, refuse an interval touching or crossing 1.00 and degenerate data, and prove N complete pairs in alternating order.
- [ ] Compare a batch fairly (plan review gap 5): if `--manifest` is chosen, aria2c 1.37.0 gets the same files through `--input-file` with `-j` set to the same connection budget, both tools verified and timed from launch to exit, and the single-file cold measurement stays reported beside it. Done when: `docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json` records both batch and single-file rows and the acceptance judges each. **Built 2026-10-31:** the bench's `batch` scenario (8 files of 4 MiB, each with its digest, 20 pairs) gives aria2c `--input-file` with each file's checksum and `-j4 -x1 -s1` (its fastest layout under four connections), checks four connections at most at the host, and keeps the single-file rows beside it. A trial through the bench's functions: fetch.py 1.72 s against aria2c's 1.12 s, paired ratio 1.540 (interval 1.514 to 1.577), every run whole: aria2c overlaps four files' round trips while fetch.py takes them in turn, so the next lever is several files of a batch at once inside the four connections.
- [x] Hold four connections with the first range in flight (found by the 2026-10-31 01:42 UTC bench, `docs/glimmer/fetch-bench.json`): two-sources held more than four simultaneous connections in 14 of 20 fetch.py runs once the first range was sent beside the `HEAD` (its connection stays open for the reader while the range workers open theirs), and gigabyte refetched 827,392 bytes against aria2c's 65,536. Done when: a test with two agreeing sources and the first range adopted counts at most four simultaneous connections at the servers under every interleaving it forces, the cause of the larger refetch is found and fixed with its test, and the bench's two-sources and gigabyte rows hold their connection and refetch gates. **Built 2026-10-31:** the bench recorded five, not six (14 of 20 runs): worker 0 read the short probe whole, closed it and opened a new connection for the rest of range 0 while the host still counted the probe; a connection read whole now carries its worker's next request to the same source (keep-alive, as aria2c does), and a kept connection the server closed gives way unspent. The refetch was the bytes unsaved at the kill: fetch.py saved only after each MiB, so it now also saves every second while any is unsaved (`SAVE_EVERY`), and the bench's `tool_saved()` waits for fetch.py's save as it waits for aria2c's (it killed fetch.py within a MiB of what was sent). Tests: four connections at most with range 0 on either host (30 of 30 under load), a held download refetching no more than a block a range, the connection carry and its fallback (131 OK twice; bench 35 OK). **Measured 2026-10-31 03:23 UTC (20 pairs):** two-sources holds four connections in 20 of 20 runs and gigabyte is met (0.991, nothing refetched against aria2c's 65,536).

## 24. A phishing-signals rubric from public guidance

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T09:29Z

Filed 2026-10-29 at the operator's question (10:00 SAST: "should we pull in phishing detection guides, docs, and articles?"). Guides and articles are not training data for Loupe: a LoRA learns from screened pages and their answers, and prose would add noise, while most articles are copyrighted and this run uses licence-checked sources only. They are worth having as a rubric: the cues that tell a lure from a real page, drawn from authoritative public guidance, cited and written in our own words, given to the screener and the Spark teacher as instructions, and turned into evaluation cases. Not a prerequisite of training: if this section stamps before §10 builds its set, Loupe 1.0 carries the rubric; otherwise Loupe 1.1 does.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review):** split. This section writes the rubric; putting it into the screener's and the teacher's prompts and measuring it (versioned prompts and data, a frozen paired experiment on held-out pages, a guidance slice, accepted, rejected and inconclusive outcomes, verified results) is §25's, after §13's evaluation runner exists. Loupe 1.0 does not wait on either.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Accept the sources. Done when: each source in `docs/glimmer/signals.md` carries its acceptance decision.
2. Write the rubric within what the screener sees. Done when: each cue names its evidence, its unavailable state, its false-positive risk and a paired genuine and lure case.

- [x] Gather the public guidance on recognising phishing pages from authoritative sources (CISA, the UK NCSC, SABRIC, APWG, the major South African banks' and SARS's own "how to spot a fake" pages) in `docs/glimmer/signals.md`, each with its URL, publisher, the date it was published or last updated, and an acceptance decision: current (updated within five years, or still served as current advice), applicable to judging a web page (not only email or calls), and its terms allowing it to be cited and paraphrased with attribution; a source failing any of these is listed as rejected with the reason (plan review gap 1). Done when: every source has its decision and reason, and no accepted source's text is copied beyond short attributed quotes.
- [x] Write the rubric in our own words in `docs/glimmer/signals.md`: each cue states the evidence it needs from what the screener receives (the page's host, its screenshot and its visible text, `buildScreenRequest` in `src/vision/page-screen.ts`), and its unavailable state, where that evidence is not on the page (a domain's age, a sender, who owns a domain); a cue whose evidence the screener never receives is listed as out of reach, and missing evidence is never evidence of fraud (plan review gap 2). Done when: each cue names its evidence and its unavailable state, and the out-of-reach cues are listed apart.
- [x] Give each cue its false-positive risk and a paired genuine and lure South African case, described in words, synthetic and brand-neutral, never a working page: a genuine third-party sign-in or payment host, a genuine urgent notice, a new business, a plain form page; and keep the screener's meanings unchanged (the four lure types, the two form flags, confidence) (plan review gap 3). Done when: every cue has its risk and both cases, and the rubric changes no answer field's meaning.
- [x] Write `test/node/glimmer-signals.test.ts` reading `docs/glimmer/signals.md`: every accepted source has its decision and date, every cue cites an accepted source, names its evidence from the screener's inputs and its unavailable state, and has its false-positive risk and both cases; no cue rests on a rejected source. Done when: the test passes and fails on a cue with no evidence, no unavailable state, no counterexample or a rejected source.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: a phishing-signals rubric from public guidance`

> **Deferred:** the rubric in the screener's and the teacher's prompts, measured on held-out pages (plan review gaps 4 to 10) -> XREF: D03 T04 §25

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-signals.test.ts` passes: every source has its acceptance decision, and every cue is sourced, within the screener's inputs, with its unavailable state, its false-positive risk and a paired genuine and lure case.

## 25. The signals rubric in the prompts, measured

Filed 2026-10-29, split out of D03 T04 §24 by its plan review (gaps 4 to 10): §24 writes the phishing-signals rubric; this section puts it into the screener's instructions and the Spark teacher's prompt and keeps it only if it measurably helps, on held-out pages, without the measurement being able to flatter it.

**Post-competition (operator, 2026-11-01 11:35 SAST):** Loupe 1.1 data work is paused until after the competition; Loupe 1.0 ships. This section waits until then.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** meta-api
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Version the prompts and the data they made. Done when: every teacher check, dataset build and served model names its prompt version.
2. Measure the rubric fairly. Done when: a frozen paired experiment's verified results decide accept, reject or inconclusive.

- [ ] Version `SCREENER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `src/vision/page-screen.ts` and the teacher's prompt (a version id and the prompt's SHA-256), record the version in each teacher check (`raw/teacher/checked.jsonl`), each dataset build's manifest and each model card, with a defined policy for pages checked under an earlier version (re-checked only on request) and a versioned dataset rebuild; inference uses the prompt its model was trained with (plan review gap 5). Done when: tests prove the Loupe 1.0 path keeps its prompt and a later version's path uses its own.
- [ ] Freeze the comparison set before the experiment: page identities, independently established labels and split membership, with the SHA-256 of every input, so neither arm (current prompt, prompt with the rubric) can change them; a page newly disputed by the teacher stays in both arms' scoring (plan review gap 6). Done when: a test proves a page the rubric's teacher disputes still counts in both arms.
- [ ] Turn §24's paired cases into an evaluation-only guidance slice: inert fixtures (host, screenshot, text), expected labels, provenance and a slice field the dataset builder keeps out of training, scored separately from the comparison set (plan review gap 7). Done when: tests prove every case reaches scoring and none enters training.
- [ ] Run the paired experiment on both consumers (Spark as the teacher, and Glimmer with the trained Loupe adapter when it exists, through §13's runner): fixed page identities, at least 300 benign and 300 lure pages overall and 100 of each South African, named model and checkpoint ids and inference settings, the current prompt as the baseline; record confusion counts overall and for South Africa, latency, and estimated and actual spend through the Meta budget (plan review gaps 4 and 8). Done when: both consumers' paired results are recorded.
- [ ] Decide by a written rule: accept when detections rise or false alarms fall with neither worse, overall and for South Africa, for both consumers; reject when either gets worse; inconclusive otherwise. Keep the current prompt in use until accepted; on rejection keep the research and the results, and restore the prompt and the versioned data (plan review gap 9). Done when: tests prove each branch, including a gain that hides a South African regression and one consumer improving while the other worsens.
- [ ] Generate the results file by machine, tied to the verified SHA-256 of every input, validated before scoring, written temp-then-rename and read back (plan review gap 10). Done when: tests fail on an altered or truncated input, a missing prediction, an invalid model answer, a wrong denominator, an interrupted write, and a detection gain bought with more false alarms.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: the signals rubric in the prompts, measured`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-signals-experiment.test.ts` passes, and the results file records the frozen experiment's verified figures and its decision.

## 26. Benign render hardening

> **Started:** 2026-10-29T11:28Z

Filed 2026-10-29 by main's decision on §6's stamp: work added to `scripts/glimmer/render.ts`, `scripts/glimmer/source-pages.ts` and `scripts/glimmer/store.ts` after D03 T04 §6's final review, while §6's render ran toward its coverage minimums, gets its own section and reviews instead of riding on §6's. Its earlier rounds, recorded in §6's findings file, are kept as written in `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s6-later-work.md` and do not count here. The render found two faults in the field: a bot-detection script that gives a hidden frame its own DOM methods returning nothing (29 good pages excluded with a TypeError), and two bot walls (HUMAN's "Press & Hold", Imperva's checkbox) the challenge check did not know (13 stored as benign pages).

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s26.md`):** prototype calls are still page-replaceable (gap 1); an unreadable frame read as no form (gap 2); wall wording missed across line breaks and past 2,000 characters (gap 3), and in frames and shadow roots (gap 4); text, flags and screenshot could come from different moments (gap 5); `recheck` could not see titles or structure, trusted unverified files, and could lose an exclusion record between removal and journal (gaps 6 to 8); `removePage` deleted before its rename was durable (gap 9); warm-up failure and slow starts were untested and a failed warm-up excluded the URL (gap 10); concurrency was tested only at parsing (gap 11). Items whose bar rose are reopened below.

**Corrected 2026-10-29 (final review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s26.md`):** the render's viewport is now 1280 by 2000, the crop the screenshot keeps, replacing D03 T04 §6's 1280 by 800, so readers and picture share one layout and what is on top can be asked anywhere in it (the header of `scripts/glimmer/render.ts` says so); a page stored without complete evidence is no longer judged by OCR of its screenshot (OCR cannot show a consent wall or a CAPTCHA is absent): it is refused when its text shows a wall, otherwise left unassessed until `render.ts recheck --rerender-legacy` renders it again; a capture with a large picture of few words is read by OCR before it is stored, and one OCR cannot read is not stored.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Keep the render's deadline for the page: a cold browser's start and first context outside it, and the batch's width chosen per run. Done when: the warm-up and `--concurrency` tests pass.
2. Read every page through the browser's own DOM methods, and survive a page with no root. Done when: the masked-frame and rootless-page tests fail before the change and pass after it.
3. Refuse every bot wall seen in the field, and take out the ones already stored. Done when: the challenge, `removePage` and `recheck` tests pass, and the real store holds no page the check refuses.
4. Recover the pages a renderer fault excluded, keeping the record. Done when: the `reopen` test passes and the real journal's reopened pages are rendered again.

- [x] Make a fresh browser's first context at launch (`warmups()` in `scripts/glimmer/render.ts`), outside every render's deadline (59b7959), and treat a failed launch or warm-up as the renderer's fault, retried in a later run, never an exclusion of the URL (plan review gap 10). Done when: `test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts` proves, with a controlled delayed start longer than the page deadline, a failed warm-up and a replaced browser, that the page's render meets its deadline, the replacement is warmed, and the URL is journalled `retry`, not `excluded`. **Done 2026-10-29:** a start slower than the page's deadline still meets it and the replacement browser is warmed (two launches, two warm-ups), and a browser that cannot open its first page raises `BrowserFault`, which `runBatch` journals as `retry` (`test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts`).
- [x] Run up to `--concurrency N` renders at once (1 to 16, 4 by default) through `parseCommand` and `runBatch` in `scripts/glimmer/render.ts` (6b90ada; plan review gap 11). Done when: `test/node/benign-urls.test.ts` proves the range, the default and each refusal, and `test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts` measures the simultaneous renders for the default and a chosen width, every URL finishing and the limit never passed. **Done 2026-10-29:** `runBatch` held exactly 4 renders at once by default and 2 when asked, finishing all ten URLs each time (`test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts`); the parser's range, default and refusals are in `test/node/benign-urls.test.ts`.
- [x] Run every in-page reader (`shownText`, `readFormFlags`, `readWalls`, the frame-visibility check and the page height) in an isolated world the page's scripts cannot reach (Chromium's `Page.createIsolatedWorld` through a CDP session, per frame), so no replaced method on an element or a prototype, and no replaced getter (`innerText`, `src`, input properties, `querySelectorAll`, CSS declaration methods), changes what is read (58594ae made the first step; plan review gap 1). Done when: the `/masked` fixture, extended to replace those methods and getters on instances and on prototypes, fails before the change and passes after it for all four readers. **Done 2026-10-29:** `readAll` in `scripts/glimmer/render.ts` runs every reader, the owner-element visibility check (`DOM.getFrameOwner`, `DOM.resolveNode`) and the height and title reads in a `Page.createIsolatedWorld` world per frame through the page's CDP session; the `/masked` and `/masked-proto` fixtures (methods and getters replaced on instances, on `Element`, `Node`, `Document` and `HTMLElement` prototypes, `Array.prototype.some` and `getComputedStyle`) read the text, the form and the overlay, and `/masked-proto` failed before the change (empty text).
- [x] Read a page that removed its own root element as a short page: the height reads 0 and the screenshot takes the viewport instead of a full-page capture that waits forever (58594ae). Done when: the `/no-root` test, which timed out before the change, passes. **Done 2026-10-29:** the `/no-root` test passes (it timed out before 58594ae); the height is read in the isolated world and a zero height takes the viewport.
- [x] Mark a frame that could not be read as unreadable, never as empty or hidden: a capture with an unreadable visible frame is retried and, if still unreadable, excluded with that reason; a detached or genuinely hidden frame still reads as hidden (plan review gap 2). Done when: tests distinguish an unreadable frame from a hidden and a detached one, and none stores a page with `has_login_form: false` while a sign-in frame was unread. **Done 2026-10-29:** a reading counts a shown frame whose evaluation failed while it stayed attached; a capture with one is tried again and then excluded as "a frame on the page could not be read" (the `/frame-churn` fixture), and a detached frame reads as gone.
- [x] Read the capture as one moment: the final URL, title, text, form flags and walls are read again after the screenshot and compared with the first reading; a change (a navigation, a wall appearing) retries the capture within the render's deadline, then excludes it as unsettled (plan review gap 5). Done when: a fixture that swaps in a wall during extraction is never stored with its earlier text and flags. **Done 2026-10-29:** the capture reads, takes the screenshot and reads again; the address, title, form flags, walls and the text's wall reading must agree, or it is tried once more and then excluded as "the page kept changing while it was captured" (the `/title-ticker` fixture); the stored labels come from the reading the screenshot sits between.
- [x] Clear an exclusion a renderer fault made with a `reopened` journal event, written by `reopenExcluded` and `render.ts reopen --matching <pattern>`, the excluded record kept and a stored page never reopened (58594ae). Done when: `test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts` proves only the matching exclusion renders again, and the real journal's 31 TypeError exclusions are reopened and rendered.
- [x] Refuse every bot wall in every place it is drawn (36f15c3 named HUMAN's and Imperva's wording in `CHALLENGE`; plan review gaps 3 and 4): wording matched with whitespace folded, in the title, the first 2,000 characters, and the text of any positioned overlay wherever it sits on the page; walls and CAPTCHA frames in nested frames and open shadow roots; a covering overlay whose content is only an image or canvas over a page with a challenge script is refused as a wall. Done when: rendered fixtures (`Press\n&\nHold`, a wall after 2,000 characters of navigation, a nested CAPTCHA frame, a shadow-root challenge, an image-only covering overlay) reach `runBatch` and nothing is stored. **Done 2026-10-29:** `wallShown` folds whitespace and reads the title, the opening, the whole text for wall-only wording, and the overlays `readWalls` collects from the page and every shown frame; an image-only cover over a bot-wall vendor's script is refused; the `/wall-split`, `/wall-late`, `/wall-frame`, `/wall-shadow` and `/wall-cover` fixtures reach `runBatch` and nothing is stored (each stored before the change).
- [x] Keep the evidence a later check needs with each stored benign page (its title and its wall readings, in the page's metadata), and audit stored pages with the same refusal rules as admission: `recheckStored` reads the full text and those fields, and a page stored before they existed is judged on its text and screenshot and, when that cannot decide, reported as unassessed, never as clean (plan review gap 6). Done when: the refusal fixtures above, stored directly, are each taken out by `recheck`, and a legacy page without the fields is reported unassessed. **Done 2026-10-29:** each benign page keeps `evidence` (its title and overlays) in its record; `recheckStored` judges a page by it with `wallShown`, a legacy page by its text and its screenshot's OCR (`render.ts recheck` reads them in batches), and reports one OCR could not read as unassessed (`test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts`).
- [x] Verify every page before auditing it: `recheck` checks each file's full SHA-256 against its metadata (`store.verify`), accounts for damaged and malformed records by id, and exits non-zero while any is unresolved; zero removals alone is not clean (plan review gap 7). Done when: tests with a mismatched file and a malformed record show both reported and the exit status failing. **Done 2026-10-29:** `recheckStored` lists an altered page and a malformed record as damaged, checks the rest, and the command exits 1 while any is damaged or unassessed (`test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts`).
- [x] Make removal and its exclusion record recoverable together: `recheck` writes a durable removal intent (the page id, its URL, the reason) before it removes the page, appends the journal's `excluded` line, then clears the intent; a rerun completes any intent it finds (plan review gap 8). Done when: tests stopping the run before removal, after removal and after the journal write each end with the page gone and exactly one `excluded` line for it. **Done 2026-10-29:** a stop after the removal and before the journal write leaves the intent, and the next recheck writes the exclusion once and clears it (`test/node/glimmer-render.test.ts`; the stop before removal is the same path with the page still there).
- [x] Make `removePage` in `scripts/glimmer/store.ts` durable in order: rename the folder to `.removing-<id>-<pid>`, sync the parent, then delete, and on open finish any `.removing-` folder left by a stop; a failed sync leaves the page whole or renamed for the next open, never half deleted (plan review gap 9). Done when: `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` proves one page goes and the rest stay, a bad id is refused, an injected failed sync and a restart each leave the page whole or absent, and unrelated pages survive. **Done 2026-10-29:** `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` sees the renamed folder still there at the parent's flush, a failed flush leaving the page out of view and renamed, and the next open finishing it with the other page whole; a bad id is refused (the test failed before the change: the flush found the folder already deleted).
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: benign render hardening after §6's final review`

Decision (unattended): file for 1.1; reversing it means doing this section before the freeze and re-running every conversion.

## 29. Names in running text the entity model misses

Filed 2026-10-31 from D03 T04 §19's completion review (gap 3, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s19.md`). §19's rules that need no model now remove every address, post box, town and signed name on the benchmark's accepted Latin pages, but a person named inside a sentence is caught only by the entity model, and the benchmark keeps 5 such names on accepted pages (the one invented name the model misses in running text). The model cannot catch its own misses, so a second, independent judge of names in running text is needed before the redaction can claim zero survivors.

**Post-competition (operator, 2026-11-01 11:35 SAST):** Loupe 1.1 data work is paused until after the competition; Loupe 1.0 ships. This section waits until then.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Measure the misses. Done when: the benchmark reports each missed running-text name's form (given name and surname, a lone first name, a name with a title) and how often each form is missed.
2. Add an independent judge and gate on it. Done when: the benchmark's accepted Latin pages keep zero unlisted names without refusing more pages than the dictionary alone, or the pages the judge cannot clear are refused and the figures say why.

- [ ] Report the running-text names the pass misses by form in `scripts/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.ts` (`docs/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.json`), with more invented names in running text than the one the current set reuses, so a single name cannot decide the result. Done when: the benchmark records missed names by form over at least 30 distinct invented names.
- [ ] Add a second judge independent of `en_core_web_lg` for names in running text (a second model of another family run locally, or a capitalised-token rule against a vocabulary of the page's own words), applied in `redact()`, `checkShown()` and `leakCheck()` in `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` and `scripts/glimmer/import-samples.ts`, pinned and part of `redactionKey()`. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts` fails the leak check on a planted running-text name the entity pass is made to miss.
- [ ] Gate the judge on the benchmark as §19 gates the pass: on only if it lowers surviving names without raising refusals. Done when: the checkpoint run records the decision and its figures, and the switch matches it.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: a second judge for names in running text`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-leak.test.ts test/node/glimmer-redact.test.ts` passes and `node scripts/glimmer/redaction-benchmark.ts` exits 0 with the judge's decision recorded.

## 31. Data-pipeline comparisons for Loupe 1.1

> **Started:** 2026-10-31T22:40:00Z

Moved 2026-10-31 from D03 T04 §13 (filed there from §9's completion review E4, §26's item 15 and §10's G13): Loupe 1.0 passed the operator's bar, so these comparisons weigh the data Loupe 1.1 is built from rather than 1.0's result.

> **Moved 2026-11-01:** the benign-render comparison to D03 T04 §38: the plan review's third gap (a replayable corpus, the rejected captures, both pipelines on identical inputs) needs a replay seam in `render.ts`'s egress, a build of its own.

> **Plan corrected 2026-11-01 (plan review, `docs/reviews/03-vision/D03-T04-s31.md`):** a label comparison needs independent per-field annotations; every figure comes from a reproducible command and its machine-readable result; the render corpus is replayable and holds the rejected captures.

**Phase:** 0
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Measure each pipeline against its conventional alternative on frozen inputs. Done when: each comparison's figures are recorded.

- [x] Build one local, reproducible comparison command, `node scripts/glimmer/compare-pipelines.ts <labels|benign|split> [--check]` (plan review gaps 2 and 3, no training and no GPU): each subcommand reads pinned inputs (their sha256 recorded), writes per-page outcomes and a machine-readable result `<store>/runs/compare/<name>.json` (atomic), and `docs/glimmer/training.md` is written from that file, never typed. Missing or partial inputs produce `incomplete` with what is missing, never a completed comparison. Done when: tests with known label errors, rejected benign pages and cross-split duplicates fail when counting, denominators or leak detection break, and an input with a page missing yields `incomplete`.

**Build order:**

1. Build the active file, the promotion, its recovery and the rollback. Done when: its tests pass.

- [x] Make the active version one stored thing that every consumer derives from (plan review gap 1): `src/vision/glimmer-active.json` (`version`, `deployment`, `previous`) is read by `glimmer-caps.ts`, which derives `DEPLOYED_VERSION`, `DEPLOYMENT`, `DEPLOYED_MODEL`, the wake's request, the screener's request and the recovery command, so a promotion is the command plus the Worker deploy that carries the file, and nothing else names a version. Done when: a test sets the active file to 1.1 and the wake request, the screening request and the recovery command all name 1.1.
- [x] Promote a version that passes the gate (`node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts promote <version>`): it takes the previous version from the verified active file (reconciled with the deployment it names, gap 3), runs `version-gate.py <version> <previous>` and refuses unless it prints `promote`, all before anything changes; refuses an already-active target, missing gate records, and a record that disagrees with the deployment. Then it admits the probe through the shared minutes ledger before any billable work, brings the new version's deployment (`isitlegit-loupe-<version>`, GPU, replicas and scale-to-zero read back through the existing checks) up beside the serving one, waits for readiness within a bound, requires one valid completion from the new model (HTTP success with no valid completion fails), settles the wake, and only then writes the active file. Done when: tests prove each refusal changes nothing, a promoted version reads back its model, an exhausted allowance and an unavailable ledger refuse, wrong settings fail, a readiness timeout fails and a non-completion fails (gap 4).
- [x] Recover from a failure after the switch began (gap 2): a promotion writes durable state (`promoting`, target, previous) before the first remote change, and the active file is written last; a lost API response, a failed probe, a failed active-file write or a killed process leaves the previous version serving and the state recorded, `promote` run again resumes or reverts it from that state, and a restoration that fails reports an unresolved state with the command to run, never that the previous version is serving. Done when: fresh-process tests recover each failure and read back the serving model and the active file.
- [x] Roll back deliberately (gap 5): `node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts rollback` restores the recorded previous deployment (still up and verified) by the same read-backs and writes the active file, so an operator who finds a problem with 1.1 returns to 1.0 in minutes without the forward gate. Done when: a test promotes, rolls back and reads back 1.0 as the active version, the request model and the deployment.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: a gate-passing Loupe version promoted onto the dedicated deployment`

## 34. Deployment minutes as a 30-minute daily average

> **Started:** 2026-10-31T20:20:00Z

Operator decision, 2026-10-31 22:16 SAST: the deployment's 30 minutes a day become an average. A busy day may borrow the next day's minutes, which then runs lighter, and unused minutes roll over; the borrowing reaches one day ahead (chosen by the operator from three offered). §14 (stamped) holds a hard 30 a day; this section changes its rule, nothing else.

**Phase:** 3
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Count the allowance through each day instead of per day. Done when: the ledger's tests prove the edges.

- [x] Put the rule in `src/vision/glimmer-caps.ts`: `AVERAGE_FROM_DAY` 2026-10-31 (the deployment's first day, §12), `BORROW_DAYS` 1, and `allowanceMinutes(day)`, the day limit times the days from the first day through `day` plus the days borrowed (60 through 2026-10-31, 90 through 2026-11-01). The day's money cap averages with it: at US$8.00 an hour the minutes bind first. Done when: `test/vision/glimmer-minutes.test.ts` (`the averaged allowance`) computes those figures and the strict rule as the same function with no day borrowed. **Done 2026-10-31.**
- [x] Count through each day in `mayCall` and in the reservation's one statement (`reserveStatement`): every day's minutes up to the day a part falls on, with the reservation's own parts up to it, may reach that day's allowance; the total cap is unchanged. A refusal is named `average-minutes` with the day and the figures. Done when: the test refuses at the edge with earlier days counted, lets a first day borrow to 60 and a day after a quiet one take what was left, checks a burst across midnight through each day, and fails if the statement counts only the day itself. **Done 2026-10-31:** `mayCall`, `the averaged allowance` and `reserveBurst on D1` in the test; counting only the day itself in the statement fails it. The wake, screener and `deploy.ts` tests moved to the new figures.
- [x] Name a refusal from every day's minutes through the reservation's last day (`throughStatement`), not only the days it touches (plan review gap 1). Done when: the test's refusal after 60 minutes on 2026-10-31 and 31 on 2026-11-01 names `average-minutes`, the day, and the figures used, requested and allowed. **Done 2026-10-31:** `names a refusal from every day's minutes through the reservation's day` in the test: "through 2026-11-01 the deployment would hold 91 of its 90 minutes".
- [x] Move the callers' tests to the new figures (plan review gap 2): `test/vision/glimmer-wake.test.ts` and `test/vision/screen.test.ts` spend the allowance with 85 minutes before refusing, so no request reaches Fireworks and Spark takes the page; `test/node/glimmer-deploy.test.ts` keeps a local wake and a Worker burst refused past one shared allowance, and one of two concurrent wakes for the last minutes. **Done 2026-10-31:** in bd9f0a2b; the wake, screener and deploy suites pass.
- [x] `deploy.ts minutes` (`ledgerStatus`) reports the allowance through today, the minutes used through today, today's held reserve and what is left. Done when: the test reads them back. **Done 2026-10-31:** `ledgerStatus` in the test.
- [x] Record the rule where the 30-minute limit is stated: `docs/specs/2026-10-26-phishing-invoice-analyser-design.md` section 11 (the budget row), `docs/specs/2026-10-26-tech-stack-design.md` (Platform services, the bulk screening row), this file's Goal, Outcome and Verification, and `docs/glimmer/training.md`. **Corrected 2026-10-31 (plan review gap 3):** the item named the tech-stack spec's section 11, which has none; the budget is the design spec's. Done when: each says 30 minutes a day on average with one day borrowed ahead, citing the decision. **Done 2026-10-31.**
- [x] Commit: `vision: the Glimmer deployment's minutes as a 30-minute daily average, one day borrowed ahead`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run --project worker test/vision/glimmer-minutes.test.ts` passes and fails if the reservation statement counts only its own day.

**Phase:** 3
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

- [x] Leave durable evidence as a local wake runs (plan review gap 1; the local path sends no gateway log and `status` holds no replica history): `reserveWake` writes `<store>/runs/wakes/<id>.json` before any request (the process id and its kernel start token), and every request window is recorded before it is sent (`touchWake`, never lowered): `askOneToken`, the key check's probe, and the whole window of work run inside a warm-up. Done when: the test kills a real wake process (`test/node/fixtures/killed-wake.ts`, SIGKILL mid-request) and reads the evidence it wrote. **Done 2026-10-31.**
- [x] Add `node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts reconcile <reservation id>` (`reconcileWake`): it settles only when the evidence's process is provably dead (no such pid, or a later process with another start token; a live or unprovable one is refused, gap 2), from the last request window plus the 6-minute tail through the ledger's own settlement (so the later of answer and deadline, shared runtime counted once, and SAST midnight split are the §14 and §34 rules, gap 3); with no evidence it changes nothing and says so (age alone releases nothing). Done when: the test shows the killed wake's headroom recovered (21 reserved, 7 kept), a live command refused, an unprovable one refused, no evidence refused, no request sent settling at 0, a burst across midnight charged to both days, and an in-flight request charged to its window. **Done 2026-10-31.**
- [x] Say what happened and read it back (gap 4): the command prints `settled`, `already-settled`, `refused` or `pending` with the minutes before and after, exits 0 only for the first two, and reports `settled` only after reading every row back settled; a settlement that could not be written is `pending` with the reservation still counted in full, and a fresh run completes it once. Done when: the test fails the settlement write, finds the reservation unchanged, then completes it from a fresh invocation without a second adjustment. **Done 2026-10-31.**
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: an abandoned deployment reservation reconciled from evidence`
