> **Started:** 2026-11-02T16:32:18Z

Filed 2026-11-01 from D03 T04 §32's completion review (excellence, out of scope). §32 switches the active version in this checkout and leaves the Worker deploy to the build session (docs/operations.md), so a release takes two commands and two sessions. The operator asked that replacing the deployed version take minutes; this section closes the second half.

**Phase:** 3
**Needs:** cloudflare
**Surface:** no

> **Plan corrected 2026-11-02 (vision lane, on START WAVE 1):** the Worker has no public screening endpoint (`src/vision/page-screen.ts`'s `PageScreen` names only its screener, and the monitor workflow is internal), so the fixture page is screened by the Worker's own screening code (`screenPage` in `src/vision/screen.ts`) configured with the production environment, and the version read back is the screener (`glimmer`) plus the active version the deployed commit carries (`DEPLOYED_VERSION` in `src/vision/glimmer-caps.ts`); a Spark-served fixture fails the release. The recorded live promotion and rollback runs need a second Loupe version to promote to, which does not exist until §47's candidate is trained and gated, so those two recorded runs are §47's promotion and rollback cycle read back here; this section builds and tests the release step now and stamps after §47 records both runs in `docs/glimmer/training.md`. Reversing this costs one public screening endpoint on the Worker.

**Build order:**

1. Build the timed release step with injected stages. Done when: its tests pass.
2. Run the release end to end and time it (with §47's cycle). Done when: a recorded release and rollback each finish inside the 7-minute budget with their stages timed.

- [x] Add `release(deps)` to `scripts/glimmer/promote.ts` with every stage injected (`preflight`, `deployWorker`, `waitHealth`, `smoke`, `screenFixture`, `servingState`, `now`) under one shared 7-minute deadline (`BUDGET_MINUTES`): preflight refuses a dirty tree, an uncommitted active-version file or a pending remote migration (read back through `wrangler d1 migrations list DB --remote`) before any deploy; it then deploys the Worker through the repository's deploy (`pnpm exec wrangler deploy --var COMMIT_SHA:<commit>`, D00 T01 §5 in `docs/operations.md`), waits for `/health` to report that commit, runs `node scripts/smoke.ts` for it, then screens one fixture page through the deployed Worker (next item) and requires it served by `glimmer` at the active version. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-promote.test.ts` shows preparation (deploy), Worker activation (`/health`), probe (smoke) and first screened page timed separately, a failure or a hanging stage at any stage stops the later stages and names the stage, the deadline expiring is reported as a timeout, a wrong `/health` commit fails, a Spark-served or wrong-version fixture fails, and a refused preflight never invokes the deploy. Cheaper substitute: trusting the deploy's exit code leaves a Worker serving the old commit or Spark silently in place of Loupe.
- [x] Prove screening on the deployed Worker, not beside it (plan review gap 1): add a `SCREEN` check to `src/smoke.ts`'s token-protected `/internal/smoke`, run only when the release asks for it (a query the plain smoke run never sends, so no other deploy pays Loupe or Spark spend), that screens one built-in fixture page through the Worker's own `screenPage` session and answers its screener and `DEPLOYED_VERSION`; the release reads that check back through `runSmoke` and fails when it is absent, Spark-served or on another version. Done when: the smoke test shows a broken Worker screening configuration failing the `SCREEN` check while a local screen would pass, and the release test refuses on each of absent, Spark-served and wrong-version answers. Cheaper substitute: screening locally with the vault's keys proves the keys, never the deployed Worker's bindings or secrets.
- [x] Say what is serving after any failure (plan review gap 3): `release` records the serving Worker version before the deploy, and on failure, timeout or interruption reads it back and reports `changed`, `unchanged` or `uncertain` with the recovery command from `docs/operations.md` (redeploy the previous commit), never success. Done when: a fresh-invocation test covers a deploy that switched traffic then failed health, one that changed nothing, and one whose state cannot be read. Cheaper substitute: naming only the failed stage leaves an operator guessing whether production moved.
- [x] Wire `node scripts/glimmer/deploy.ts release` to the real stages: wrangler deploy of this checkout, `/health` on `https://isitlegit.derick-f5a.workers.dev`, `runSmoke` from `scripts/smoke.ts` (including its `SCREEN` check), the migration and tree preflight, and the serving-state read-back through wrangler. Done when: usage and a bad argument refuse before any deploy, and the command prints the stage timings and the version read back. Cheaper substitute: a shell script joining the four commands loses the per-stage timing and the read-back checks.
- [ ] With §47, record one promotion release and one rollback release run live, each inside the 7-minute budget, in `docs/glimmer/training.md` with preparation, probe, Worker activation and first screened page timed separately. Done when: both records name their commit, their active version before and after, and their figures. Cheaper substitute: recording only the promotion leaves the rollback path, the one an incident uses, unproven live.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: a release and a rollback carried through to the live Worker`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-promote.test.ts` passes with the release step's cases, and the recorded runs are in `docs/glimmer/training.md`.

## 41. Day-zero case capture into the training store

> **Started:** 2026-10-31T20:26:27Z

Filed 2026-10-31 from the operator's lane brief at `~/isitlegit-data/loupe-brief.md`. Owning layer: offline ingestion in `scripts/glimmer/`. Production is read-only; no product, schema or retention changes. Capture is private and never authorises publication or model submission.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** cloudflare
**Surface:** no

**Corrected 2026-10-31 (Argon plan review):** Verified the findings against `src/db/migrations/0051_findings_by_run.sql`, `0045_feedback.sql`, `0016_intake.sql`, `0039_case_uploaded_by.sql`, `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` and the current store reader. The review's symbol locations do not all match this checkout; the corrections below follow the actual source. No implementation has started.

**Environment gate:** Restore `cloudflare` to Needs once the owner decision is recorded; remote reads require the existing Wrangler login.

**Coordinator decisions 2026-10-31 21:44 SAST (loupe-inbox.md):** (1) Binary and raster artefacts that cannot be cleared of recipient identifiers are withheld; their hashes, types and counts are kept. No binary sanitiser this version. (2) Verdict mapping: `malicious` without `false_alarm` takes the phishing privacy rule; every other verdict takes the genuine rule. A later `false_alarm` makes the case genuine mail: its stored revision is redacted in place to the genuine rule (the record, hashes and labels stay; only recipient-identifying text is replaced), and no unredacted copy remains in quarantine or elsewhere. That is redaction, not deletion, so the never-delete rule holds; Argon's proposed purge is rejected. This supersedes the earlier quarantine wording. Demo priority stands: policy never blocks the demo path.

**Capture contract:**

- Recipient scope comes from `cases.forwarded_by`, `cases.uploaded_by`, the matching user name/email, original and outer recipient headers, inline-forward sender identity and MSG recipient properties. Organisation identity supplies context, never a reason to erase unrelated attacker brand names or all organisation domains. The recipient dictionary applies to metadata, MIME parts, nested messages, evidence, feedback and parsed/visible text; missing identity prevents certification.
- Only fixed, single-statement SELECT query templates are exposed by the D1 adapter, with bounded keyset pagination and no caller-supplied SQL. R2 reads use only case-owned indexed artefact keys. Read current-run evidence and check outcomes with `COALESCE(cases.run_id, cases.id)`, retaining explicit run identity. Double-read source metadata around downloads and retry changed snapshots.
- The revision fingerprint hashes canonical sorted case fields, current-run evidence/check outcomes, verdict, all artefact descriptors and all feedback rows, plus recipient dictionary digest and redaction policy/code key. Do not depend on `cases.updated_at` for feedback. R2 digest mismatches fail capture; missing objects remain retryable even when metadata is unchanged.
- Parse MIME and JSON structurally. Apply existing redaction and the entity pass to personal text fields; retain separate typed sender-domain, authentication-result, attachment-hash and link signal fields. Recipient identifiers in a URL take precedence over byte-exact URL preservation, with that transformation recorded. Never apply the prose redactor to an entire JSON document, digest or authentication record. Re-render cleared HTML/text inertly and verify the resulting text and image using the existing render and OCR checks; existing raster or binary data is not declared cleared by a text-only check.
- Resolve the external root before writes and reject repository-contained or symlink-escaped destinations. Capture uses its own private schema outside the page store and never calls page import or training submission. Prove that the existing page enumerator and dataset builder ignore capture records; admission to any model pipeline remains a separately authorised consent and redaction gate.
- Write `capture/1.1/stats.json` and `capture/1.1/STATS.md` atomically from each case's latest committed revision only. Category and lure type remain null unless an explicit validated source field supplies them; do not infer ground truth from free text. Count metadata inventory separately from successfully retained payloads, with failure and withholding denominators and current-production coverage distinct from historical retained cases.
- Use a local user systemd service/timer with `OnBootSec` and daily persistent scheduling, a single-instance lock, bounded retries and a content-free last-run status under `capture/1.1/`. Its wrapper pins cores 0 and 1, refuses below 3000 MB available RAM and records deferred execution. Test lock contention, memory deferral, command failure and status publication. Scheduler installation waits for coordinator answers and a successful private dry-run/readback.

**Build order:**

1. Define the capture contract in `docs/glimmer/case-capture.md`. Done when: source tables, object keys, privacy handling and retry semantics are mapped against the current producer code.
2. Build `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts`. Done when: each case has an atomic, versioned private record and repeated unchanged runs add nothing.
3. Prove recovery in `test/node/glimmer-capture.test.ts`. Done when: interrupted captures recover without publishing incomplete records or losing prior captures.
4. Install a local daily and startup runner documented in `docs/glimmer/case-capture.md`. Done when: a real read-only run reports coverage and the next scheduled execution is verified.

- [x] Map D1 tables and R2 producers in `docs/glimmer/case-capture.md`. Done when: cases from every intake channel, evidence, raw mail, page renders, attachment types and hashes, verdicts and member feedback have explicit source fields, pagination and missing-object handling; unknown classifications remain null. Cheaper substitute: raw mail alone loses the investigation signals.
- [x] Implement bounded read-only collection in `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts` using remote Wrangler D1 SELECTs and R2 reads. Done when: all cases are paginated, payloads never enter logs or command arguments, failed reads remain retryable, and changed verdicts or feedback produce a new revision. Cheaper substitute: a time-only watermark misses later feedback.
- [x] Apply the privacy policy in `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts` with `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts` and the existing entity pass. Done when: phishing captures retain signals while removing the recipient's name and address throughout content and metadata; genuine mail uses existing redaction, preserving sender domains, headers, authentication, links and attachment hashes; images and binary formats that cannot be safely cleared are explicitly withheld and counted, never silently certified. Pending and ambiguous verdicts take the genuine policy; demo accounts follow the same policy. Cheaper substitute: redacting only visible body text leaves recipient identifiers in headers and artefacts.
- [x] Persist captures beneath a new `capture/1.1/` folder in the external training store from `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts`. Done when: restrictive permissions, safe generated paths, a single-writer lock, bounded temporary files, atomic manifest publication and content hashes protect each revision; unchanged reruns add nothing and existing versions are never deleted or changed, except the one permitted change: the in-place redaction of a case's stored revisions to the genuine rule after a later `false_alarm` (coordinator decision above). Cheaper substitute: marking a case captured before its objects exist loses data on interruption.
- [x] Redact a case's stored revisions in place to the genuine rule when a later `false_alarm` arrives, from `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts`. Done when: the record, hashes and labels stay, recipient-identifying text is replaced in every earlier revision, no unredacted copy remains in the store, quarantine or temporary files, and the redaction is written atomically and recorded. Cheaper substitute: quarantining the old revision keeps the identifiers on disk.
- [x] Write a one-page private stats report from `scripts/glimmer/capture-cases.ts`. Done when: email, page and attachment counts by channel, verdict, category and lure type reconcile with committed manifests, with case coverage, missing, withheld and failed counts, and no personal text. Cheaper substitute: total cases alone hides missing objects.
- [x] Prove the contract in `test/node/glimmer-capture.test.ts` with synthetic data only. Done when: pagination, all channels, changed feedback, recipient redaction, benign redaction, binary withholding, protected-account parity, path traversal, interrupted writes, read failures, overlapping runs and unchanged reruns each have a failing regression assertion. Cheaper substitute: mocks that bypass collection and persistence cannot prove end-to-end capture.
- [x] Document and enable the daily/startup invocation in `docs/glimmer/case-capture.md`. Done when: the local runner retries failures, performs a startup sweep before the 30-day expiry, and a private live report records actual coverage without storing case content in the repository; disabling the runner preserves the store forever. Cheaper substitute: a manual command alone does not meet daily capture.
- [x] Commit: `loupe: capture day-zero cases into the private training store`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-capture.test.ts` passes; a second unchanged capture adds zero revisions and the private report reconciles captured, missing and failed cases.

## 42. A pinned download mode for fetch.py

Filed 2026-11-01 from D03 T04 §27's item on the digest-or-pin rule (finding 4): the checker already admits a pinned file with no published digest (`check_pin()` in `scripts/glimmer/inputs.py`), but the downloader cannot fetch one, because `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py` needs a digest to verify a download, so the brief still sends a source with no digest to `REJECTED-2.md`. In the 2026-10-31 sweep that alone refused `crimson` (https://github.com/pragseclab/Crimson). Owning layer: the downloader, offline ingestion in `scripts/glimmer/`.

**Post-competition (operator, 2026-11-01 11:35 SAST):** Loupe 1.1 data work is paused until after the competition; Loupe 1.0 ships. This section waits until then.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no
**Risk:** light

**Build order:**

1. Add the mode and prove it admits only pins. Done when: its tests pass.

- [ ] Add a pinned mode to `scripts/glimmer/fetch.py` (`--pin <immutable ref>` in place of `--sha256`/`--md5`): the file is fetched to the partial as now, its SHA-256 is taken from the bytes at the end, and the file is published only when the pin passes `check_pin()`. Done when: `tests/scripts/test_glimmer_fetch.py` publishes a pinned file with no published digest, records our SHA-256 beside it, and refuses a moving branch name and a rerun whose bytes differ from the SHA-256 first recorded. Cheaper substitute: trusting the first download without recording its digest lets a later run swap the file.
- [ ] Teach `docs/glimmer/astra-collection-brief-2.md` and `scripts/astra-collect.py` the pinned download line. Done when: `test/node/astra-brief.test.ts` fails the brief without it, and a source with an immutable pin and no digest is no longer sent to `REJECTED-2.md` for that reason alone.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: a pinned download mode for sources with no published digest`

**Test checkpoint:** `python3 -m unittest tests.scripts.test_glimmer_fetch` passes with the pinned cases.

## 45. The Loupe 1.1 dataset

> **Started:** 2026-11-03T15:08:01Z

Filed 2026-11-01 from §31's measured findings and §13's miss causes, under the operator's 1.1 approval. Brand labels were wrong on 43 percent of judged pages (§31), so brand is never taught or scored as truth in 1.1; brand or host is a grouping key only. Loupe 1.0 flagged 19 of 20 unseen-brand benign pages in §13's sorting, so 1.1 carries hard benign negatives for unseen brands. Target, measured later by the training writer: false alarms at most 2 percent and lure recall above Muse Wonder's on the same held-out pages. This section builds and gates the dataset; it does not train.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Stop teaching brand. Done when: 1.1 training and evaluation lines carry no brand as an answer or a score, while grouping still uses brand and host.
2. Make the split class-aware and grouped. Done when: no brand, site, template or near duplicate crosses the sides, and each side's lure share and lure-type shares match the build's targets within the stated tolerance, with §39's completion gaps closed (training-side type counts and the build's own targets reported).
3. Add hard benign negatives. Done when: benign pages of brands absent from the lure training side (a brand's own pages, small real sites, news and fan sites naming a brand) are present in training in recorded numbers.
4. Freeze, build and gate. Done when: `sources.ts freeze --version 1.1` and `build-dataset.ts 1.1` produce the dataset with 1.0 held-out protection intact, and the version gate records its figures.

- [ ] In `scripts/glimmer/build-dataset.ts`, for version 1.1 only: the taught answer carries no brand (brand null is not taught as truth either; the 1.1 line's answer omits brand scoring), evaluation never scores brand, and `labelTrusted` brand trust plays no part; brand and host remain grouping and capping keys only. 1.0's published dataset stays byte for byte unchanged. Done when: tests build 1.1 from pages with wrong brands and find no brand taught or scored, groups still held whole, and 1.0's files unchanged.
- [ ] Make 1.1's split class-aware: whole groups assigned so training and evaluation each match the target lure share and lure-type shares within 5 share points, reported per side with the build's own targets, closing §39's recorded gaps for the real split (not only the baseline). Done when: tests with imbalanced groups report UNMET rather than silently passing, and the real build's summary records both sides' type counts.
- [ ] Select hard benign negatives for 1.1: benign pages whose brand is absent from the lure side, a brand's own pages, small real sites, and news or fan sites naming a brand, kept in training in recorded numbers and never held out as the only copy of their group. Done when: the build summary counts them by kind and a test finds an unseen-brand benign page in training.
- [ ] Freeze and build: `node scripts/glimmer/sources.ts freeze --version 1.1`, then `node scripts/glimmer/build-dataset.ts 1.1`, with `previousHeldOut()` and `previousHeldOutItems()` intact. Done when: the build refuses a missing or shortened 1.0 held-out list, no 1.0 held-out page or item trains 1.1, and the summary records counts, hashes and the hard-negative numbers.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: the Loupe 1.1 dataset, brand as grouping only`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-dataset.test.ts test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` passes and the 1.1 summary records its split balance, hard negatives and hashes.

## 46. Muse Wonder teacher pilot

> **Started:** 2026-11-03T15:08:02Z

Filed 2026-11-01 under the operator's 1.1 approval. The teacher is Muse Wonder 1.0 at medium through Muse Code only (`muse exec`), never the Meta Model API. Labels are lure or not, lure type, capture validity and reasons; never brand as truth. Only public or redacted pages go to the teacher; never unredacted client mail, never anything from `samples/`. Spend cap stated before every paid call: the pilot's cap is recorded in the run file before it runs. **Corrected 2026-11-03 (operator and plan review):** Muse Code usage is exhausted after the pilot and initial Wonder batches (`raw/wonder/`), so subsequent teacher and judging passes use the headless Codex teacher (`codex exec`, via `scripts/glimmer/codex-astra-label.ts` and `scripts/glimmer/codex-terra-label.ts`, tested in `test/node/glimmer-codex-astra-label.test.ts` and `test/node/glimmer-codex-terra-label.test.ts`) with the same schema and token-usage accounting; Wonder (`wonderRefusal`) permits only `public` and `benign` origins (refusing all `samples` origins and `/samples/` paths before `muse exec`), whereas the headless Codex teachers (`terraRefusal`) additionally permit redacted store-derived sample pages (`origin: "samples", redacted: true`) for judging Microsoft-confirmed disputed sample lures while refusing unredacted sample pages (`origin: "samples", redacted: false`), any `/samples/` filesystem path, other origins and missing screenshots before `codex exec`.

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Build the labelling tool. Done when: every answer validates against its schema before it is stored.
2. Run the 20-page pilot. Done when: its speed and quality are reported to the coordinator and the historical pre-batch approval sequence and pinned 20 pilot records verify.

- [x] Write `scripts/glimmer/wonder-label.ts` (and replacement headless Codex teacher drivers `scripts/glimmer/codex-terra-label.ts` and `scripts/glimmer/codex-astra-label.ts`): `wonder-label.ts` labels one page a batch item through `muse exec --model muse-wonder-1.0 --reasoning-effort medium --contributor-mode off --image <screenshot> --output-schema <schema> --prompt-file <prompt>`, validates each answer against the schema (lure, lure type, capture validity, reasons; no brand field as truth) before storing it under `<store>/raw/wonder/`, and refuses a page with no screenshot, a non-public/non-benign origin, or any path under `samples/` before invoking `muse exec`; `codex-terra-label.ts` and `codex-astra-label.ts` validate answers against the same schema (`scripts/glimmer/wonder-schema.json`), record token usage from `codex exec --json`, and refuse unredacted sample pages, `/samples/` paths, non-permitted origins and missing screenshots before invoking `codex exec`. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-wonder-label.test.ts`, `test/node/glimmer-codex-terra-label.test.ts` and `test/node/glimmer-codex-astra-label.test.ts` stub `muse exec` and `codex exec`, verify refusal before execution, refuse invalid answers without storing, and store valid ones with usage read-back. **Done 2026-11-03:** all three driver suites pass (15 tests total).
- [x] Verify the 20-page pilot (`PILOT_PAGE_IDS` and `PILOT_HARD_NEGATIVE_IDS` in `scripts/glimmer/wonder-label.ts`: 10 source lures and 10 unseen-brand hard benign negatives drawn with seed 20261101 from the 1.0 dataset's meta), its recorded metrics in `docs/phase-runs/2026-11-01-loupe-1.1-dataset.md` (19.7 seconds a page, 394.657 seconds total, 20 of 20 valid answers, 0 disagreements with source labels, 2 invalid captures `ariyadasa-2021-a552983ead340ed9` and `look-for-mallory-1bb42be49daeeb96`), and the historical pre-batch approval sequence (the pilot completed with no large run started, followed by the coordinator's 18:06 SAST go before Batch 1 and the subsequent Codex Terra/Astra teacher passes). Done when: `verifyPilotRecords` verifies all 20 pinned pilot records, their hard-negative composition, timing, valid-answer denominator and disagreements, and the pilot and Codex teacher figures are recorded in `docs/phase-runs/2026-11-01-loupe-1.1-dataset.md`. **Done 2026-11-03:** `verifyPilotRecords()` passes against `<store>/raw/wonder/` and in `test/node/glimmer-wonder-label.test.ts`.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: Muse Wonder labels for Loupe 1.1, pilot first`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-wonder-label.test.ts test/node/glimmer-codex-terra-label.test.ts test/node/glimmer-codex-astra-label.test.ts` passes and `verifyPilotRecords()` verifies the pilot's 20 pinned labels, hard-negative composition, timing, valid-answer rate and disagreements against the schema.

## 47. Twelve to seventeen thousand more training pages

Operator, 2026-11-02: grow the training store from its frozen 172,987 pages (1.1 freeze, 2026-11-02) by 12,000 to 17,000 source pages, to a total of 180,000 to 190,000 pages for the next Loupe version. Same rules as every earlier sweep: sources with a recorded licence in `docs/glimmer/sources.md`, collected through `scripts/astra-collect.py` under the collection brief, no sample-derived pages to any teacher, and the store stays outside the repository (`GLIMMER_DATA_DIR`).

**Phase:** 7
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Collect, label and freeze the new pages. Done when: the store's frozen total reads between 180,000 and 190,000 pages with every new source's licence recorded.

- [ ] Name the new sources in `docs/glimmer/sources.md` with licence and expected yield before collecting. Done when: each source has a licence row. Cheaper substitute: collecting first and licensing later, refused.
- [ ] Collect through `scripts/astra-collect.py` into `~/isitlegit-data/glimmer` until the frozen total (the 1.1 freeze plus this sweep) reads 180,000 to 190,000 pages. Done when: the freeze record prints the total. Cheaper substitute: counting raw captures before deduplication, refused (the freeze count is after exact screenshot deduplication, as in 1.1).
- [ ] Label the new pages by the 1.1 pipeline's own rules (trusted source lure labels first, the pinned judge for disputes), never a sample-derived page to a teacher. Done when: the build-side completeness recount shows no unlabelled new page unaccounted for.
- [ ] Rebuild through §43's one command and record the new version's counts in `docs/glimmer/training.md`. Done when: the record names pages, sources and the split.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: twelve to seventeen thousand more training pages`

**Test checkpoint:** the freeze record's total is between 180,000 and 190,000 and `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-sources.test.ts` passes.

## 48. Confidence escalation from Loupe to Spark

> **Started:** 2026-11-02T16:45:45Z

Operator, 2026-11-02: a page Loupe is unsure about escalates to Muse Spark 1.3, and a page Spark is unsure about escalates to a second Spark pass, so a borderline page is never settled by the weakest answer. Section 1's failover already sends a page to Spark when Loupe cannot answer; this section adds escalation when Loupe answers but is unsure. Muse Wonder 1.0 is not a tier: it is the Muse Code reviewer on the operator's subscription, not a Meta Model API product model, and the product provider rule (Meta Model API plus Fireworks only) stands, so Wonder's part is offline judging in §46. Owning layer: the screening route in `src/vision/screen.ts`.

**Phase:** 6
**Needs:** meta-api
**Surface:** no

> **Plan corrected 2026-11-02 (vision lane):** the escalation is recorded without a D1 migration (migration numbers are the coordinator's): `screenPage`'s result gains `tier` (`glimmer`, `spark`, `spark-second`), `escalation` (`loupe-unsure`, `spark-unsure` or none) and every Spark call reference, and the monitor's existing `glimmer_reason` column stores `unsure` for a Loupe answer that did not settle the page, so D05 T04 §3's stored record and the eval read the same fact from what §1 already stores. The band measurement is computed from the monitor set's recorded Loupe and Spark answer lines (`eval/glimmer/monitor-pages/runs/`), with the second pass's pages counted and priced at Spark's recorded mean cost; no new paid run is needed to measure tier one, and a band that raises false alarms on that set does not stay at its default.

**Build order:**

1. Escalate on a confidence band, then prove each tier in tests. Done when: a stubbed unsure Loupe answer reaches Spark, an unsure Spark answer reaches the second pass, and a sure answer at either tier stops.

- [x] Add an unsure band to `screenPage` in `src/vision/screen.ts`: a Loupe answer whose `confidence` falls inside `ESCALATION_BAND` (a named constant, defaults 0.35 to 0.65) escalates to Spark through the same `structured(PageAnswer, ...)` call §1 uses, and the returned `PageScreen` names the tier that settled it. Confidence is `P(lure)` everywhere (the screener prompt's definition): a sure lure answer is 0.9, a sure benign answer 0.1, and both stop. Done when: `test/vision/screen.test.ts` shows a sure Loupe answer (0.9, 0.1) never calling Spark and a band answer (0.5) always calling it, with the band read from the one constant. Cheaper substitute: escalating on every answer doubles Spark spend for no measured gain.
- [x] Teach the same confidence in `answerOf` in `scripts/glimmer/build-dataset.ts` (plan review gap 1): a lure teaches 0.95 (0.7 when unverified), a benign page 0.05 (0.3 when unverified), so a trained Loupe's confidence can fall in the band at all; today it teaches 0.95 or 0.7 whatever the label, and 1,000 recorded held-out answers hold no band value. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-dataset.test.ts` reads a benign page's taught confidence below the band and a lure's above it. Cheaper substitute: routing on a confidence the training never taught asserts a signal that does not exist.
- [x] Add the second Spark pass in `src/vision/screen.ts`: when Spark's own answer also falls inside `ESCALATION_BAND`, ask once more and take the second answer, recording both call references in the result. Done when: the test's unsure-then-sure Spark script settles on the second answer and a sure first Spark answer is never re-asked. Cheaper substitute: averaging the two answers invents a confidence neither model gave.
- [x] Define the terminal outcomes (plan review gap 3): a second Spark answer still in the band settles the page (tier `spark-second`); a Spark failure at either pass (budget, timeout, invalid output, provider) returns the typed failure with `glimmer_reason: unsure` and every Spark call reference preserved, and no page ever takes more than two Spark calls through escalation. Done when: tests cover two unsure Spark answers settling, a failing second pass, and the two-call bound. Cheaper substitute: retrying until sure spends without bound on the pages least likely to settle.
- [x] Record the escalation in the screening result (`screener`, the tier, and why the page escalated) where §1 records the failover reason, so the monitor's stored record (D05 T04 §3) and the eval's ablation read the same fact. Done when: `test/vision/screen.test.ts` reads the tier and reason back from the result for an escalated and a non-escalated page. Cheaper substitute: logging the escalation only loses it to the ablation.
- [x] Persist the tier in the monitor's stored record (plan review gap 2; migration number from the coordinator, requested 2026-11-02 in `vision-outbox.md`): `monitor_screens` gains the settling tier and the first Spark call reference, written by `src/monitor/screen.ts` beside the settling call it already stores. Done when: a monitor test reads the tier, the escalation and both call references back from D1 for a two-pass page, and a plain failover page reads back distinct from an escalated one. Cheaper substitute: result-only recording loses the fact where the ablation and the monitor actually read.
- [x] Measure the band on recorded answers with exercised coverage (plan review gap 4): from the held-out set's recorded Loupe and Spark answer lines (1,000 pages, `runs/eval-1.0/`) and the §1 monitor set's (40 pages), record Loupe alone, Loupe plus escalation, Spark alone, accuracy, lure recall, false alarms and Spark cost per page in `docs/frontier-log.md`, counting escalations exercised at each tier. Done when: the rows state their coverage, a tier with zero exercised pages is recorded as unmeasured (never as passing), and the band stays at its default only if escalation raises neither false alarms nor lowers recall where it was exercised. Cheaper substitute: pricing hypothetical second passes asserts an accuracy no run measured.
- [x] Commit: `vision: escalate unsure Loupe answers to Spark`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/vision/screen.test.ts` passes, and fails if a sure Loupe answer reaches Spark or an unsure one settles without escalating.

## 49. Every screened page captured and labelled

> **Started:** 2026-11-02T17:25:54Z

Operator, 2026-11-02: anything new sent to IsItLegit is captured for the next training set and labelled immediately. The capture half already exists privately in §41 (day-zero case capture, its own schema, a separately authorised consent and redaction gate before any model pipeline); this section is the admission half for pages: every page `screenPage` settles, in the monitor and in a case, is copied into the page store under a new origin, labelled at once by Spark (the §9 teacher), and queued for offline judging when the label is disputed. Muse Wonder 1.0's part is that offline judging (with Astra, as §9's queue already does), never a production call. No page trains until the §41 gate admits it. Owning layer: offline ingestion in `scripts/glimmer/`, with the copy made where the page is labelled (the D05 T04 §10 flow for monitor findings).

**Phase:** 6
**Needs:** meta-api
**Surface:** no

> **Plan corrected 2026-11-02 (vision lane):** §41's gate has no record file of its own for pages, so this section names it: `<store>/production-admission.json` (`admitted` page ids, written only by the authorised admission action §41 requires) is the one record the dataset builder reads for origin `production`. The settling provenance (tier, settling answer, Loupe's answer when it escalated) rides on the page record as an optional `capture` field, so no second store is invented. The monitor and case callers that hand settled pages to `captureScreenedPage` are D05 T04 §10's flow (trust lane); this section builds the store, labelling, queue and admission it calls. A page with recipient identifiers (the `EMAIL`, `PHONE` and `LONG_NUMBER` shapes in `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts`) in its host or text is withheld at build time and counted in the build's dropped figures, never silently dropped and never redacted into training by this section.

**Build order:**

1. Copy each settled page at labelling time, label it, queue disputes. Done when: a labelled page is in the store with its label and provenance, a disputed one is in the review queue, and neither trains before admission.

- [x] Write `captureScreenedPage` in `scripts/glimmer/store.ts`: for a settled screening (the full `Screened` result of §48, its tier and call references, plus the page's screenshot, text and host), store the screenshot and visible text under origin `production` with host, settle time, settling tier and the settling and Loupe answers when escalated, deduplicated by screenshot hash as §10 does; it makes no model call. A settled page with no screenshot (text-only mode) is not stored and is returned counted as `no-screenshot`, never silently missing. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts` stores one copy per page, none for a page already stored, reads the tier and answers back, and counts a text-only page. Cheaper substitute: recapturing the page later gets a changed or deleted page, not the one that was judged.
- [x] Clear a captured page before any model pipeline touches it (plan review gap 1): labelling and judging refuse a page `<store>/production-clearance.json` does not name by id with its screenshot and text hashes (the §41 authorised consent and redaction clearance, content-bound so a changed page loses it), and a page whose host or text carries recipient identifiers (`EMAIL`, `PHONE`, `LONG_NUMBER` in `scripts/glimmer/redact.ts`) cannot be cleared until redacted, its raster withheld meanwhile. Done when: the store test shows an uncleared capture causing zero labeller calls, and a capture whose bytes changed after clearance refused. Cheaper substitute: checking identifiers at build time sends uncleared production content to Spark and the judges first.
- [x] Label each cleared captured page at once with Spark through the §9 teacher path (`scripts/glimmer/teacher-label.ts`), the label written as `label_source: spark-teacher` beside the settling answers, as a production-label mode that writes complete validated labels and never touches a ground-truth page; a failed or refused labelling leaves the page counted unlabelled and a rerun labels the backlog without repeating a successful call. Done when: the store test reads a Spark label on every cleared captured page, a failed labelling leaves the page counted unlabelled, never guessed, and a rerun makes no second successful call. Cheaper substitute: taking Loupe's own answer as the label trains Loupe on itself.
- [x] Queue a captured page for offline judging in the store's `review-queue.jsonl` when Spark's label disagrees with the settling answer or Spark's confidence falls inside `ESCALATION_BAND` (§48), judged by Astra and Wonder offline before admission, as §9 queues disputes; judged labels come back through a validated import (`applyJudgements`) that refuses a judgement whose page hashes no longer match, and only an imported judgement takes a page out of the queue. Done when: the test's disagreeing page lands in the queue unchanged, a judged page carries the judge's label as its own `label_source`, and a stale judgement is refused with the page still queued. Cheaper substitute: admitting disputed labels unjudged imports the teacher's errors at scale.
- [x] Admit captured pages to a training build only through §41's consent and redaction gate: `admitProductionPages` writes `<store>/production-admission.json` atomically for cleared, labelled, undisputed (or judged) pages only, with withdrawal by the same writer; the dataset builder takes origin `production` pages only when that record names them, and a page with recipient identifiers in its URL or text is withheld and counted in the dropped figures, as §41 rules. Done when: `test/node/glimmer-capture.test.ts` shows an unadmitted production page in no training line, an admitted eligible page in a training line through the real build, and a withheld page counted, never silently dropped. Cheaper substitute: importing captures directly bypasses the consent the capture was taken under.
- [x] Report the flywheel in `docs/glimmer/training.md`: captured, labelled, queued, judged, admitted and withheld counts per version. Done when: the counts reconcile with the store manifest for one run. Cheaper substitute: a captured total alone hides the unlabelled and disputed backlog.
- [x] Commit: `glimmer: capture and label every screened page`

**Test checkpoint:** `pnpm exec vitest run test/node/glimmer-store.test.ts test/node/glimmer-capture.test.ts` passes, and fails if a settled page is missing, unlabelled without being counted, or admitted unjudged after a dispute.

## 50. Loupe 1.1 promoted on a better-than-1.0 gate

> **Started:** 2026-11-02T18:02:00Z

Operator, 2026-11-02: this is a competition, so the only gate for Loupe 1.1 is that it is better than Loupe 1.0; if it is, 1.1 ships before submission. This supersedes the 2026-11-01 note pausing 1.1 until after the event for promotion only (§42 to §47 stay Phase 7). Better means, on the §13 held-out set with its stratified grouped split (§39): higher lure recall at no more false alarms, or fewer false alarms at no less recall, each with its denominator printed. Owning layer: the promotion gate in `scripts/glimmer/version-gate.py` and the one stored active-version file §32 built.

**Phase:** 6
**Needs:** none
**Surface:** no

**Build order:**

1. Compare 1.1 against 1.0 on the held-out set and promote on the one gate. Done when: the gate prints both versions' recall, false alarms and denominators, and 1.1 is the active version exactly when it is better.

- [x] Amend `decide()` in `scripts/glimmer/version-gate.py` to the operator's one gate: on the same held-out file with complete records, pass exactly when lure recall is higher with false alarms no higher, or false alarms lower with recall no lower; the more-training-lines rule and the separate South African slice rule stop being gates (the slice still prints, with its denominators). Done when: `tests/scripts/test_version_gate.py` passes a strictly-better candidate on each improvement branch, refuses an equal one, refuses a recall gain bought with more false alarms, promotes a candidate trained on fewer lines that is strictly better, promotes a strictly better overall candidate whose South African slice is worse, and the command prints both versions' recall, false alarms and denominators on acceptance and on refusal. Cheaper substitute: keeping the extra rules lets a better 1.1 stay undeployed on a technicality, which the operator's decision forbids.
- [ ] Take the candidate only from its measured handoff (plan review gaps 1 and 2): Loupe 1.1's training and evaluation are the loupe lane's sections (D03 T04 §38 and §39 on `loupe-1.1`, records `runs/loupe-1.1` and `runs/eval-1.1` in the shared store beside 1.0's, on the same held-out file the gate's `eval_sha256` equality already proves). Done when: missing, incomplete or differently-measured candidate records stop this section as unready (never recorded as a failing gate), and only complete records on the same file reach `decide()`. Cheaper substitute: comparing records from different held-out files or an unfinished run manufactures a verdict no measurement supports.
- [ ] Promote a passing 1.1 through §32's stored active-version file and §36's release path, recording both versions' figures in `docs/glimmer/training.md` and the model card (§17). Done when: the active version reads 1.1 after a passing gate and 1.0 after a failing one, with the figures recorded either way. Cheaper substitute: deploying the new weights by hand leaves the stored version and the running deployment disagreeing.
- [ ] Close §36's live proof in this section's cycle (plan review gap 5 on §36): on a passing gate, record §36's timed promotion release (1.0 to 1.1), then its timed rollback release (back to 1.0), then promote and release 1.1 again as the final serving state; on a failing gate no promotion or rollback is run, the gate is never bypassed, §36's live records stay unwritten and §36 stays unstamped until a later passing candidate. Done when: `docs/glimmer/training.md` holds both timed records and the final active version, or the failing gate's figures with 1.0 still active. Cheaper substitute: leaving the rollback unrecorded leaves §36 unprovable and the incident path untested live.
- [ ] Commit: `glimmer: promote Loupe 1.1 on a better-than-1.0 gate`

**Test checkpoint:** `python3 -m unittest tests.scripts.test_version_gate` passes, and fails if an equal or worse candidate promotes.

- The monitor screens pages on the fine-tuned Glimmer deployment, falls over to Muse Spark in Standard mode, and records each page's screener, reason and cost.
- The deployment's running time, warm-up included, never passes 30 minutes a day on average in the ledger (one day borrowed ahead, §34), and it reads 0 replicas 5 minutes after its last request.
- `docs/glimmer/sources.md` and `docs/glimmer/training.md` account for every training source, licence, count and decision, and no sample-derived content is in the repository.
- `pnpm test` passes; `python3 scripts/todo-graph.py validate` and `plan --check` pass.
